# TempestOS v0.26.0 — Owner test pack for Addenda A and B

| | |
|---|---|
| Tester | Steven Kreczman |
| Branch | Addendum A: `release/v0.26.0` (PR #31 into `main`, draft). Addendum B: `v0.26.0/addB-4-workflows`, not yet merged into the release branch |
| Written against | Addendum A: `release/v0.26.0` at `510f337c` ("graphify update"), after the Blue rounds 1 and 2 and every colour-team report were merged. Addendum B: `v0.26.0/addB-4-workflows` at `5a8ba277` ("Addendum B B-4: index E9, M9 and the §B7 chain packs; DECISIONS DB-05 to DB-07"), which holds B-0 to B-4. B-5 (S8) is not in it. |
| Version file | `0.26.0` |
| State | Addendum A: ready for owner testing. Addendum B: B-0 to B-4 built; S8 pending (B-5); owner testing not started. Not released. Not tagged. Not merged to `main` (DA-01). |
| Platform | Windows, PowerShell |

**What this pack covers.** Addendum A: the twelve calculators M4, M5, M6, S4, S5, S6, B4, B5, B6, E4, E5 and E6, result linking (L1) with the Saved calculations list and the Calculation packs panel, the five linked chains, reference records with the licence lock, the independent check, and the sixteen v0.26.0 base templates they build on. Addendum B: the fourteen calculators M7, M8, M9, E7, E8, E9, S7, S9, B7, B8, B9, F1, F2 and F3, the Fluid Power discipline, the material library (W3), the CAD parameter import (W1) and the scan deviation import (W2), the shared gas-law and tolerance-stack kernels, and the ten §B7 chains (seven are built-in packs). **S8 (lifting lugs and spreader beams) is not in the tree this pack was written against.** It is behind a feature flag and is being finished on another branch (B-5). Its script (T-S8), its chain script (T-C11) and the review checklist in section 10 are drafts, marked *to be confirmed when B-5 merges*. Sections 1 to 9 follow the order the addenda ask for (§A8, §B10). Section 10 is the S8 review checklist. Section 11 is a case built on one of your own hydraulic jobs.

**How to use it.**

- Work top to bottom, or follow the order in section 9.
- Every script has a **Result** column. Leave it blank until you test. Write `P` (pass), `F` (fail), `B` (blocked) or `N` (not run), and a short note.
- Expected numbers come from the repository's own known-answer (KAT) cases and from running the real calculators headless on those cases laid over a new sheet's defaults, which is what the form does. Most KATs are analytic: they were computed independently, not copied from a book. A pass here shows the build matches its own specification. Section 4 lists the 29 KATs that still need a primary source (13 from Addendum A and the base set, 16 from Addendum B), the six that are also Secondary, and the sources nobody has pinned yet.
- The form shows results to about five significant figures; the governing bar shows the utilisation to three. The KAT tolerances are tighter. Where they are tighter, compare with the *Form shows* column.
- A new sheet opens on the template's declared defaults only. Tables are empty. Some values have no default at all. A blocked sheet says what to enter next. Every script says what a new sheet shows before you type.
- Standards are cited by clause or regulation number and title only. This pack contains no standards text and no table values from BS 7671, BS EN 60204-1, IEC, CIBSE or MCS, and none from EN 10270, EN 13906, ISO 13788, BS 5422, ASME B31.3, ASME BTH-1, EN 13155, AS568, ISO 3601 or the Parker handbooks. The only table-like numbers are test values that the repository marks as invented (the E4 ampacities and factors, the bearing `Y` of 0.8, the cable tables, the climate and the motor nameplate of the Addendum B examples) or illustrative (the B4 layer conductivities).
- Warnings are not failures. Record the ones you see.
- Names such as `L1 motor` are yours to choose; use the ones in the script so the steps read the same.

## 1. Build and run on Windows from a clean clone

**You need.** Windows 10 or 11 x64, Git, and the .NET SDK. `global.json` pins SDK `10.0.302` with `rollForward: latestFeature`, so any 10.0.3xx SDK or later feature band of 10.0 builds it. No workloads, Visual Studio, Node, Python, Docker, database or licence file are needed. The network is needed once, for `dotnet restore`.

**Steps.**

```powershell
# 1. Check the SDK
dotnet --list-sdks

# 2. Clean clone of the Addendum B branch (keep it out of any other working tree)
cd $HOME
git clone --branch v0.26.0/addB-4-workflows https://github.com/kreczmans-creator/TempestOS-Core.git TempestOS-Core
cd TempestOS-Core
git log -1 --format="%h %s"      # expect 5a8ba277 or later (Addendum A alone: release/v0.26.0 at 510f337c)

# 3. Locked restore, as CI does (fails if a lock file does not match)
dotnet restore src/TempestOS.slnx --locked-mode

# 4. Release build with warnings as errors, as CI does
dotnet build src/TempestOS.slnx --configuration Release --no-restore -p:TreatWarningsAsErrors=true
```

Expected: `Build succeeded.` with `0 Warning(s)` and `0 Error(s)`.

**Automated tests** (the Calc suites run in seconds; run them before you touch the app):

```powershell
foreach ($p in 'Engine','Sheets','Library','Calculators','FluidPower','Import') {
  dotnet test "tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.$p" --configuration Release --no-build
}
dotnet test tests/Tempest.Desktop.Tests --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~CalcSheets"
dotnet test tests/Tempest.Core.Tests --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~BoltShearReconciliationTests"
```

| Suite | Expected | Source of the figure |
|---|---|---|
| `Tempest.Calc.Tests.Engine` | 449 pass | run on Linux while writing this pack, final code of `5a8ba277` |
| `Tempest.Calc.Tests.Sheets` | 135 pass | same |
| `Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library` | 337 pass, 1 skipped (D-32, crash mid-create) | same (338 listed) |
| `Tempest.Calc.Tests.Calculators` | 1502 pass | same |
| `Tempest.Calc.Tests.FluidPower` | 88 pass | same (new for Addendum B) |
| `Tempest.Calc.Tests.Import` | 562 pass | same (new for Addendum B) |
| Desktop `CalcSheets` filter | 127 cases run on Linux: 123 pass, **4 fail on a clean checkout** (the E9 and M9 worked-example PDF tests, which find no committed `sheet.pdf`; section 7.7 RR-B3) | run on Linux; the Avalonia suite ran headless, so no window was opened |
| Core `BoltShearReconciliationTests` | all pass | 3 test methods with 7 data rows by a static count |

The two calculator suites that Addendum A listed grew: `Calculators` from 869 to 1502 cases (the shared suites now run over 42 templates), `Library` from 168 to 337, `Engine` from 384 to 449, `Sheets` from 111 to 135. I did not run a warnings-as-errors release build on Windows. The build line above is the CI line; I expect `0 Warning(s)`.

Optional full suites. Core: `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Core.Tests --configuration Release --no-build`. Desktop: `$env:TEMPEST_TEST_TIMEOUT_FACTOR = '3'` then `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Desktop.Tests --configuration Release --no-build` (it takes several minutes and opens no window). The last logged full-suite figures predate Addendum A and Addendum B (`docs/build-log/STATUS.md` still reads Core 7,221, Desktop 1,193, Calc 849), so do not compare your totals with them. Also read the latest *Build & Test* results on PR #31. `STATUS.md` cites a green Windows run only for `6afead3`, which is earlier than Addendum A.

**Run the app.**

```powershell
# Close any running instance first (one instance per data folder)
Get-Process Tempest.Desktop, Tempest.Harness -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Stop-Process

# Use a dedicated data folder so the test never touches other data
$root = "$HOME\TempestOS-Test-Data"
dotnet run --project src/Tempest.Desktop/Tempest.Desktop.csproj --configuration Release --no-build -- --persistence-root $root
```

- The title bar reads `TempestOS <version> (<commit>)`. The commit must match `git log -1 --format=%h`. If it does not, you are running a different build.
- To start again from nothing, close the app and delete `$root`.
- No CI step launches the windowed app on Windows (`PHYSICAL_REVIEW.md` §1). Your launch is the first Windows run of this build. Note any window problem separately from the calculator results.
- Create or open a project (rail: Projects) before the linking, pack and record scripts. Links, packs and the Saved calculations list work inside one project. With no project open, nothing is listed and a pack cannot be created.

**Where the calculators are.** Rail: Engineering. Then Modules → Calculations → Worksheets. The same worksheets also sit under Disciplines → (discipline) → Worksheets, and Ctrl+K finds each by name. The older calculators are under Calculations → Calculators.

**The worksheet form, in one paragraph each.**

- *Left column.* The catalogue, then two panels. **Saved calculations** lists the open project's calculations as `name · template title · rev A · status`, with an amber ` · STALE` at the end of a stale one, and has an **Open** button. **Calculation packs** has a **Pack template** picker, a **Pack title** box, **Create pack from template**, **Packs in this project**, **Calculations in the selected pack** with **Open pack member**, a **Check the pack** block (the checker, read-only and set to the signed-in person; **Checker's organisation**, **Check statement**, the outcome picker, **Check pack**, **Reopen pack**) and an **Issue the pack** block (**Issue reference**, **Client**, **Issue pack**).
- *Right column.* Each input is a row `symbol — description`, a value, a unit picker, a source picker (manual, drawing, spec, assumption, evidence, reference, link) and a source reference. The *Recalculating…* marker shows while the sheet updates. Below the inputs are the governing bar (PASS or FAIL with the utilisation, or `Blocked — …`), the results, the checks, the notices and **Show working**. A stale calculation shows an orange `Stale — …` banner, and **Update linked inputs** (a draft) or **Update to revision n** (a checked one) beside it.
- *Buttons.* **Save as calculation** (it reads **Save changes** once saved), **Record check**, **Issue**, **Revise**, **Export inputs (CSV)**, **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** and, on the base plate, **Attach external report…**. A saved draft also shows the **Link an input to another calculation** row (**Link input**) and, if its template declares record kinds, the **Reference records** panel (**Apply record**, **Save inputs as record**).
- *Tables.* Table inputs have no row editor in this release (D-37). They arrive by **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** from the clipboard, as JSON or as the CSV the form exports. An import re-seeds the whole form, so import first and type the single values afterwards.
- *Open over unsaved edits.* If the form holds edits that are not saved, the first press of **Open** only says `The form has unsaved changes. Save them first, or press Open again to discard them and open '<name>'.` A second press for the same calculation discards them.

## 2. Status

Status words: **built** (in the tree, passing, to the Definition of Done in §A3), **partial**, **dropped**. Nothing is partial or dropped. Source of the status: `docs/build-log/TASKS.md`, `STATUS.md` and the code at `5a8ba277` (the Addendum A rows were first written at `510f337c`). The version column is the template version in the code at `5a8ba277`. Several rose during Addendum A, and seven older ones rose again during Addendum B (section 8). DA-24 notes that a draft saved on an older template version no longer runs.

**Test counts.** "Own tests" gives the number of `[Fact]` (F) and `[Theory]` (T) methods in the calculator's own test class, then the number of xUnit cases they produce, counting each theory row (from `dotnet test --list-tests` on the Calculators project, run on the final code). Every template also runs the shared suites: `CommonCalculatorTests` (378 cases across the 42 templates: known answers, well-formedness, defaults, hostile numbers, empty tables, golden sheet, hash, render time), `CsvRoundTripTests` (64, every worked example), `WorkedExampleTests` (64), the Red and hostile-input suites and the performance budgets. "KAT cases" is the number of `TestCases` in the template. "Unconfirmed KAT" is the number of those tagged `CalcReferenceStatus.Unconfirmed`, counted from the code.

### 2.5 Addendum B foundations and workflows

| Item | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Fluid Power discipline (ADR-0170) | built | The fifth calculation discipline and the sixth engineering domain. `CalcDiscipline.FluidPower` is appended to the enum, so no template hash changed. Core objects (Circuit, Actuator, Power unit) carry typed attributes and no design-code check. Pneumatics (ISO 6358) is out of scope and has no placeholder. `Tempest.Calc.Tests.FluidPower` (88 cases) and `FluidPowerDisciplineTests` 8. |
| W3 material property library | built | Materials are reference records of kind `material.properties`: E, G, ν, ρ, α, λ, f_y, f_u, R_m(d) curve coefficients, permissible strains, secant-modulus points, friction pairs and temperature tables read linearly, never extrapolated. Each value carries its own source, edition, page or table, licence class, who entered it, when and an evidence SHA-256. Four generic **indicative** seed materials ship. A calculation pins the material revision. Library `Materials/*` 48 cases (`MaterialLibraryTests` 29). **No form panel of its own**: the Reference records panel applies a material (T-W3). |
| B6 gas-law kernel | built | `Tempest.Calc.Engine.Physics.GasLaw`. B6 uses it with sheet and numbers unchanged; F2 uses it. Engine `GasLawTests` 29 cases. |
| M1 stack engine as an API | built | `ToleranceStackEngine` in the Engine. M1 uses it with sheet and numbers unchanged; F3 and W2 call it. M1 2.1.0 adds a `sigma` column that W2 fills. Engine `ToleranceStackEngineGuardTests` 14; Calculators `ToleranceStackEngineTests` 19. |
| W1 CAD parameter import (ADR-0171) | built | `Tempest.Calc.Import`. NX `.exp` and `.xlsx`, Creo text, SolidWorks equations and design tables, Onshape CSV and JSON, generic CSV and JSON. An expression is never evaluated. File cap 5 MB. Desktop: **Import CAD parameters…** on a saved draft. Import project 562 cases in all; Library `CadDrivenToleranceChainTests` 10, `CadImportSecurityTests` 12; Desktop `WorksheetCadImportTests` 3 and `WorksheetCadImportSecurityTests` 7. Not a template: no hash, no sheet. |
| W2 scan deviation import (ADR-0172) | built; the PDF table path is **not built** (dropped under the §B2 drop order: export the table as CSV or `.xlsx`) | Reads ZEISS or GOM Inspect tables, multi-stage tables, PolyWorks and Geomagic text through your own column profile; mean, σ, Cp, Cpk, Pp, Ppk and an Anderson–Darling check; pushes a fitted normal into M1, F3 or M2. Desktop: **Import scan deviations…**. Library `ScanToSealChainTests` 11; Desktop `WorksheetScanImportTests` 4 and `WorksheetScanImportReviewTests` 6. Not a template. |
| Licensed values stay withheld through links | built | A value linked from a calculation that holds an input pinned to a licensed record is withheld from the inputs export and printed on the issued sheet as the link plus "value derived from a licensed record — withheld". Library `LicensedLinkTests` 8. |

### 2.6 The ten §B7 chains

Nine of the ten chains exist in this tree as tests; seven are built-in pack templates. Chains 2 and 3 are workflows run through the import panels, not packs. Chain 6 needs S8.

| Chain | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 1 Hydraulic power unit F1 → F2 → B1 → E1 → E9 → E4 → E3 → E5 | built | `pack.hydraulic-power-unit` (8 steps, 20 links). Needs your E4 tables, so the form cannot create it (T-C6 part A). Library `HydraulicPowerUnitChainTests` 12. |
| 2 Scan-to-seal W2 → M1 → F3 | built (workflow) | No pack template. Library `ScanToSealChainTests` 11 (T-C7). |
| 3 CAD-driven tolerance W1 → M1 → M2 | built (workflow); M8 is not in the test | No pack template. Library `CadDrivenToleranceChainTests` 10 (T-C8). The §B7 line also names M8; the test links M2 only. |
| 4 Mechanism review M9 → M7 → M4 → M5 → E1 → E4 | built | `pack.mechanism-review` (6 steps). The cable step opens blocked until you give it your tables. `MechanismReviewChainTests` 9. |
| 5 Actuated linkage M9 → F1 → F2 | built | `pack.actuated-linkage` (3 steps). `ActuatedLinkageChainTests` 7. |
| 6 Lifting beam S8 → S7 → S4/M6 → S1 (flag on in test only) | **pending B-5** | Nothing in this tree. To be confirmed when B-5 merges (T-C11). |
| 7 Envelope moisture B4 → B7 ← B2 | built | `pack.envelope-moisture` (3 steps). The wall layers are typed in both B4 and B7: there is no per-layer link. `EnvelopeMoistureChainTests` 9. |
| 8 Cold pipe B1 → B9 ← B2 | built | `pack.cold-pipe` (3 steps). B1 gains an optional `OD` column and the `OD_1` and `T_line` ports. `ColdPipeChainTests` 10. |
| 9 Pipework supports B8 → S2 → S6 (+ PROFIS placeholder) | built | `pack.pipework-supports` (3 steps). B8's moment is not carried. `PipeworkSupportsChainTests` 8. |
| 10 Electronics enclosure E7 → E6 → E2 → M8 / S9 | built | `pack.electronics-enclosure` (5 steps). E7 loss is an E6 table cell; E2 gives M8 and S9 the breather-limited pressure and force. `ElectronicsEnclosureChainTests` 12. |

Seven Addendum B packs plus the five Addendum A packs make **12 built-in pack templates** (`CalcPackTemplates.All`). Each has an issued worked example under `docs/worked-examples/packs/` (13 folders with the older bracket assembly). `ChainPackWorkedExampleTests` 12 and `PackExampleInputsTests` 13 (they were 5 and 8 for Addendum A). Every chain test proves values with units on each link, that each binding is a real link, stale propagation from the first step to the last, that Check and Issue are refused while stale, and a check and issue of the pack as one bundle with an independent checker.

## 3. Manual test scripts

### 3.0 How the scripts work

Each per-calculator script follows one pattern.

1. Open the worksheet (`Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets`) and read the governing bar before you type: a new sheet opens on the declared defaults only, and the script says what the bar reads (`Blocked — …` with the next thing to enter, or the reading of the defaults).
2. Load any table inputs by import (PowerShell block, then **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**). Do this first.
3. Type or pick the single values listed, then wait for *Recalculating…* to clear.
4. Compare the results, the governing bar and the checks with the expected column.
5. Run the variation steps. Each one changes an input and has an expected FAIL, a block, or a changed number.

**Inputs.** Every case is the first Confirmed known-answer case of the calculator (its `TestCases`). The expected values are that case's `CalcExpectedValue` list, with its relative tolerance. The *Form shows*, governing-bar, check and notice texts come from running the calculator on the case laid over a new sheet's defaults.

**Governing bar format.** `PASS — <check name>: utilisation <u>` or `FAIL — …`, with `u` to three significant figures. A utilisation at or below 1 passes.

**Typical notices.** Tabulated inputs raise a warning of the kind "a tabulated value not taken from a reference record". That is expected until you apply a record (section 5). It is a warning, not a failure. The sheet prints the repeated ones as one line listing the symbols; the form keeps one per symbol.

**Cell format.** A table cell with a number carries a unit (`"Unit":"mm"`). A text cell carries `"Text"`. A yes/no cell carries `"Flag"`. The JSON in the scripts is exactly the form the import reads. A blank cell is the same as no cell.

**Sources and Check.** A check or an issue is refused until every input has a source other than *manual*. Values you type in a per-calculator script are *manual*, so those scripts stop before Check. The worked examples carry sources on every input, so T-00, T-L1, T-IND and T-REC use them. The packs' example inputs carry the source `assumption: example input — replace with your own`.

**Addendum B scripts.** The scripts of 3.B and 3.D follow this pattern and add three things. Each opens with the **new-sheet reading**, which is what the governing bar shows before you type anything. Each ends with an **expected-output table** that gives the tolerance of the known-answer case and what the form shows. Where the tolerance is tighter than the form's rounding, the *Form shows* column is the test. Each has **variation steps** that change one input and give a FAIL, a block or a changed number, and then put the input back. Four template versions rose after the first draft of this pack (E5 to 1.2.0, B4 to 1.2.0, and E1 and E2 to 1.4.0, with M1, B1 and B2 also higher, section 8). Each rise added an output or a port and changed no number in the Addendum A scripts. The reference line of a sheet now reads the new version.

### T-00 Common worksheet flow (save, list, reopen, export, check, issue, revise)

Run this once, on M5, with the worked example `skf-6206-pump`. That example is tagged Unconfirmed in section 4. Here it tests the flow, not the book value.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open a project. Open **Rolling bearing life (ISO 281)**. In PowerShell from the repository root run `Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.bearing-life\skf-6206-pump\inputs.json \| Set-Clipboard`, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. | Status reads `Imported 15 input(s).` Governing bar reads `PASS — Modified rating life against required life: utilisation 0.659`. `L_nmh` is about 7590 h (7589.55 computed). | |
| 2 | Type `T00 bearing` in **Calculation name**. Press **Save as calculation**. | Status reads `Saved as 'T00 bearing' in <project name>; it is in the Calculation register and has its own Evidence.` The watermark reads DRAFT. The button now reads **Save changes**. **Record check** appears. | |
| 3 | Look at **Saved calculations** (left column). | One row: `T00 bearing · Rolling bearing life (ISO 281) · rev A · Draft`. No ` · STALE`. | |
| 4 | Press **Export inputs (CSV)**. Paste into Notepad. | Status reads `Copied 19 input row(s) as CSV to the clipboard.` The first line is `symbol,value,unit,provenance,reference,revision`. | |
| 5 | Press **Record check**. | Status reads `Checked. Issue when ready.` Watermark CHECKED. **Issue** appears. If the build refuses because the checker is the author (a global Evidence setting can demand this), record `B` and note the message. | |
| 6 | Press **Issue**. | Status reads `Issued as 'T00-bearing-A'. The sheet is attached to its Evidence and listed under Reports.` Watermark ISSUED. The form is read-only: `This calculation is Issued; it is read-only. Revise it to start a new revision.` | |
| 7 | Find the issued sheet. Look in Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Calculation register, then in the project's Evidence. Open the PDF. | The PDF opens. The Inputs section cites each source. The Verification footer shows the real template hash and the known-answer cases passed (5/5 for M5). `STATUS.md` records PDFs under 200 KB; note the size if you can see the file. Note where you found it (section 7.3). | |
| 8 | Back in the form, press **Revise**. | Status reads `Revision B started as a draft; revision A is superseded.` The form is editable again. The Saved calculations list shows both: `… · rev A · Superseded` and `… · rev B · Draft`. | |
| 9 | Pick another worksheet in the catalogue. Pick `T00 bearing · … · rev A · Superseded` in **Saved calculations** and press **Open**. | The M5 form returns with the saved inputs. Status reads `Opened 'T00 bearing' · rev A · Superseded. It is read-only; revise it to start a new revision.` | |
| 10 | Pick the `rev B · Draft` row and press **Open**. Change `F_r` to 3 kN. Without saving, pick the `rev A` row and press **Open**. | Status reads `The form has unsaved changes. Save them first, or press Open again to discard them and open 'T00 bearing'.` The form still shows rev B with 3 kN. | |
| 11 | Press **Open** again on the same `rev A` row. | The edit is discarded and rev A opens, as in step 9. | |

### T-M4 — Shaft: static, fatigue, keys, critical speed (`mech.shaft@1.1.0`)

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-simply-supported` — Uniform shaft, central load and mass: slope, deflection, critical speed. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see section 4 for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

This is a deliberate FAIL: a uniform 30 mm shaft with a 2 kN load and the form's default limits (slope 0.001 rad, deflection 0.13 mm) breaks both limits. Fatigue and yield pass. The stated limits are the form defaults, not requirements.

### T-E4 — Conductor sizing and overload coordination (`elec.conductor-sizing@1.1.0`)

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-three-phase-mcb-c` — Three-phase 7.5 kW (output) load on a type C MCB, θ_a 35 °C with C_a 0.91, two circuits with C_g 0.77. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see section 4 for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

**Synthetic data.** The capacities and the factors C_a 0.91 and C_g 0.77 are invented for the repository's tests. They match no published table. Never use them for a design. The ratings are the typical preferred series. Replace them with your own records for UK practice (section 5).

This calculator demands an independent check (DA-12). Whoever created the calculation, or set or changed any of its inputs, cannot check it. See the last step and T-IND.

**Values to enter** (a new sheet opens on the declared defaults only; caption is `symbol — description`):

| Input | Value | Unit | On a new sheet |
|---|---|---|---|
| `P` — Rated output (shaft) power; η converts it to input (DC: electrical power drawn, η not applied) | 7.5 | kW | already set |
| `U` — Supply voltage (line voltage for three-phase) | 400 | V | already set |
| `cos_phi` — Power factor (not used for DC) | 0.85 |  | already set |
| `eta` — Efficiency (not used for DC) | 0.9 |  | already set |
| `theta_a` — Ambient temperature | 35 | degC | type it |
| `N_g` — Grouped circuits | 2 |  | type it |
| `C_a` — Ambient correction factor | 0.91 |  | type it |
| `C_g` — Grouping correction factor | 0.77 |  | type it |
| `C_i` — Thermal insulation correction factor | 1 |  | already set |
| `supply` — Supply | three-phase |  | already set |
| `device` — Protective device | MCB type C |  | already set |

**Table inputs.** A new sheet opens with every table empty and the form has no row editor (D-37), so load these by import. Copy this block to the clipboard from PowerShell, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**:

### T-E5 — Fault protection: Zs, disconnection, adiabatic, fault level (`elec.fault-protection@1.2.0`)

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-mcb-b32` — 32 A type B MCB, 20 m of 2.5/1.5 mm² copper at 70 °C. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see section 4 for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

A 32 A type B MCB on 20 m of 2.5/1.5 mm² copper at 70 °C, U_0 230 V. Every value equals the form default. The sheet takes the disconnection time as 0.1 s (check line `t_d = 0.1 s vs t_req = 0.4 s`) and warns that no let-through I²t is entered, so it uses I_f²·0.1 s, which is conservative.

This calculator demands an independent check (DA-12). Whoever created the calculation, or set or changed any of its inputs, cannot check it. See the last step and T-IND.

**Values to enter** (a new sheet opens on the declared defaults only; caption is `symbol — description`):

| Input | Value | Unit | On a new sheet |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Z_e` — External earth fault loop impedance | 0.35 | ohm | already set |
| `L` — Circuit length | 20 | m | already set |
| `S_line` — Line conductor cross-section | 2.5 | mm^2 | already set |
| `S_cpc` — Protective conductor cross-section | 1.5 | mm^2 | already set |
| `I_n` — Rated current | 32 | A | already set |
| `device` — Protective device | MCB type B (I_a = 5 I_n) |  | already set |

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-ih-moment-bolts` — The 260×256 I-section with N = 300 kN, M = 100 kN·m, V = 80 kN; two bolts a row, bolt rows at 165 mm from the axis, no prying. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see section 4 for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

260×256 I-section on a 400×400×20 plate, S275, C25/30, N 300 kN, M 100 kN·m, V 80 kN, two bolts a row at 165 mm, no prying. Three values differ from a new sheet: `alpha` (the case uses 1.5, the form opens on 1), and `m` and `l_eff`, which have no default at all and must be typed (the sheet is blocked until you do). Check the rest against the list below.

This calculator demands an independent check (DA-12). Whoever created the calculation, or set or changed any of its inputs, cannot check it. See the last step and T-IND.

**Values to enter** (a new sheet opens on the declared defaults only; caption is `symbol — description`):

| Input | Value | Unit | On a new sheet |
|---|---|---|---|
| `alpha` — α, concentration factor √(A_c1/A_c0) of the foundation (1 when the foundation geometry is not known) | 1.5 |  | type it |
| `m` — Bolt to flange-weld toe distance, tension T-stub (required: it follows from x_b, the column and the weld) | 28 | mm | type it |
| `l_eff` — Effective length of the tension T-stub, total for the n_t bolts (required: from the cl. 6.2.6.5 patterns for the plate) | 200 | mm | type it |
| `column` — Column section type | I/H |  | already set |
| `h` — Column depth, along the plate length L (I/H and RHS) | 260 mm |  | already set |
| `b` — Column width, along the plate width B (I/H and RHS) | 256 mm |  | already set |
| `t_f` — Flange thickness (I/H) | 17 mm |  | already set |
| `t_w` — Web thickness (I/H) | 10 mm |  | already set |
| `B` — Plate width | 400 mm |  | already set |
| `L` — Plate length | 400 mm |  | already set |
| `t_p` — Plate thickness | 20 mm |  | already set |
| `f_y` — Plate yield strength | 275 MPa |  | already set |
| `f_ck` — Concrete characteristic cylinder strength | 25 MPa |  | already set |
| `alpha_cc` — α_cc, long-term effects on the concrete strength | 0.85 |  | already set |
| `gamma_c` — γ_c, partial factor for concrete | 1.5 |  | already set |
| `gamma_M0` — γ_M0, partial factor for the plate | 1 |  | already set |
| `gamma_M2` — γ_M2, partial factor for the anchor bolts | 1.25 |  | already set |
| `N_Ed` — Axial force, COMPRESSION positive (the opposite sign to S3's N_Ed, which is tension positive; S2's R1_y, an upward reaction, is compression positive) | 300 kN |  | already set |
| `M_Ed` — Major-axis moment (uniaxial) | 100 kN*m |  | already set |
| `V_Ed` — Shear force | 80 kN |  | already set |
| `n_t` — Holding-down bolts in each bolt row (one row each side) | 2 |  | already set |
| `n_b` — Holding-down bolts in total (both sides) | 4 |  | already set |
| `x_b` — Bolt row distance from the column axis (outside the column face, inside the plate edge) | 165 mm |  | already set |

| `C_fd` — C_f,d, friction coefficient plate to grout | 0.2 |  | already set |

T-IND shows the second person succeeding. | |

Typing a made-up reference with source *evidence* does not do this: the Library refuses an evidence source that does not point at a file attached to the calculation's own Evidence. | |

### T-B4 — U-values and room heat loss (`bs.room-heat-loss@1.2.0`)

**Inputs from** known-answer case `ka-cavity-wall` — Insulated cavity wall, a window and natural ventilation. Status: Confirmed (analytic; see section 4 for what is and is not checked against a primary source).

The layer conductivities in this case are illustrative inputs, not design data (the spec says so). The window has its own U (1.4). Natural ventilation. The `elements` table has an optional `flow` column (blank = horizontal; `upward` for a roof or ceiling, `downward` for a floor) that sets the internal surface resistance; this case leaves it blank. The roof case at the end of the script uses it.

**Values to enter** (a new sheet opens on the declared defaults only; caption is `symbol — description`):

| Input | Value | Unit | On a new sheet |
|---|---|---|---|
| `theta_int` — Internal design temperature | 21 | degC | already set |
| `theta_e` — External design temperature (location record) | -3 | degC | already set |
| `U_target` — Target U for built-up elements without their own target | 0.3 | W/(m^2*K) | type it |
| `n` — Air change rate | 0.5 | 1/h | already set |
| `V` — Room volume | 40 | m^3 | already set |

```powershell
'@ | Set-Clipboard
```

| 8 | Read the notices under the checks. | Warnings, not failures. Expect one notice per symbol of the kind `<symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (<record kind>); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.`, for `n` (Emitter exponent). Record any other notice you see. No *Block* line appears. | |
| 9 | Tick **Show working**. | Numbered steps appear, each as formula, substitution, result, with the clause tag (« …) where the step cites one. **Go to step** jumps to the governing step. | |
| 10 | Variation — Select a smaller emitter. Re-import the table with `Phi50` 1400 W (JSON below), then confirm `V_pipe` 20 L and `V_plant` 10 L. | Governing bar reads `FAIL — Selected emitters against room heat (governing room): utilisation 1.07`. `u_emit` shows 1.0714. `V_s` stays 38 L. `V_e` shows 2.4959 L and `p_i` shows 0.79019 bar. | |

```powershell
'@ | Set-Clipboard
```

### T-IND Independent check (E4, E5, S6; DA-12)

This script needs two signed-in people. **Settings → Switch person…** lists the released people the People directory knows a sign-in identity for, and asks you to confirm by name (there is no password). The v0.21.0 owner runbook (`docs/releases/v0.21.0/PO Test Runbook.md`, B2 and C13) adds a person under Engineering → Reference data → *Add a person*, then **Release**. I did not establish whether a person added that way carries a sign-in identity. If **Switch person…** lists nobody, record `B` on the steps that need it and rely on `IndependentCheckTests` and the `BlueRoundTests` cases named below.

E5 is used because its worked example carries a source on every input, so a second person's check is not stopped by the source gate. Person A is whoever is signed in now; person B is the second person.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| I1 | As A, open **Fault protection: Zs, disconnection, adiabatic, fault level**. Copy `docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.fault-protection\workshop-radial-c16\inputs.json` to the clipboard and press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. Name it `IND check`. **Save as calculation**. Press **Record check**. | Status reads `Imported 18 input(s).` then the save line. The check is refused: `'IND check' needs an independent check: sign in as someone other than its author (<A>) to check it.` Governing bar before the save reads `PASS — Loop impedance within maximum (disconnection in time): utilisation 0.979` (README). | |
| I2 | Settings → **Switch person…** → B → confirm. Return to the worksheet form, pick `IND check` in **Saved calculations**, press **Open**, then **Record check**. | Status reads `Checked. Issue when ready.` B did not create or edit the inputs. | |
| I3 | As A again (switch back), import the same JSON, name the sheet `IND edit`, **Save as calculation**. Switch to B. Open `IND edit`. Change `Z_e` to 0.7 ohm and press **Save changes**. Press **Record check**. | Status reads `Saved.` Then the check is refused: `'IND edit' needs an independent check: sign in as someone other than anyone who set up or changed its inputs (<A>, <B>) to check it.` B edited an input, so B is now an author. | |
| I4 | Switch to A and press **Record check** on `IND edit`. | Refused with the same text. The set of authors travels with each revision. A third person is needed. Record `B` if you cannot add one. | |
| I5 | Automated: run `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~IndependentCheckTests\|FullyQualifiedName~BlueRoundTests"`. | Pass. The cases include `R_A_05_Whoever_Changed_The_Inputs_Cannot_Check_Them_…`, `R_A_05_The_Pack_Check_Refuses_Whoever_Changed_A_Members_Inputs`, `R_A_11_Nobody_Signed_In_Is_Not_An_Independent_Checker` and the two `P_A_03` cases: a person who only presses **Update to revision n** (re-pins a link) is **not** made an author by it. | |

### T-L1 Result linking by hand: bind, dimension safety, cycle, stale marker, update (E1 and M4)

This script links two calculations yourself in the form, then walks the stale marker and the revision update. The five chains (T-C1 to T-C5) do the same at pack scale. The worked examples carry sources for every input, so the calculations can be checked and issued. Their inputs load by import. Run these from the repository root:

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.motor-sizing\ball-screw-pick-and-place\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # E1: 20 inputs
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.shaft\gearbox-input-shaft\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard                  # M4: 25 inputs
```

Name it `L1 motor`. **Save as calculation**. | Status `Imported 20 input(s).` then `Saved as 'L1 motor' …`. Results include `T_rms` 1.4402 N·m, `n_pk` 9000 rpm, `T_pk` 2.579 N·m and `P_rms` 784.14 W. Governing bar `PASS — RMS torque SF·T_rms ≤ T_rated`, utilisation 0.900. | |

Name it `L1 shaft`. **Save as calculation**. | Status `Imported 25 input(s).` then `Saved as 'L1 shaft' …`. `R_A` 3.19531 kN and `n_f_min` 1.69. Governing bar `PASS — Slope at the bearings against limit`, utilisation 0.896. | |
| A3 | Under **Inputs** find the *Link* row (**Link an input to another calculation**). Pick input `T`, calculation `L1 motor · rev A · Draft`, output `T_rms`. Press **Link input**. | Status `Linked T to T_rms; it is pinned to that calculation's current revision.` Field `T` shows about 0.0014402 kN·m (the form converts N·m to the input's unit). Its source shows `link`. | |
| A4 | Link `n` to `L1 motor` output `n_pk`. | Status `Linked n to n_pk; …`. `n` shows 9000 rpm. `n_f_min` rises to 2.1017; the fatigue check reads u 0.714. | |
| A5 | Pick input `n`, calculation `L1 motor`, output `T_rms`. Press **Link input**. | Refused: `'L1 motor'.T_rms is in N·m (m²·kg/s²); 'n' needs rpm (1/s). The dimensions differ, so the link is refused.` The link on `n` is unchanged. | |

### 3.C The five chains (A7): create a pack, follow the values, break an upstream input, mend it, check, issue

**How the chain scripts work.** Each script runs in the **Calculation packs** panel of the worksheet form. You need an open project. **Use a new project for each chain script**: calculation names are unique in a project, and several chains share step names (for example `Motor sizing` and `Enclosure heat`). A second pack in the same project is refused with `The project already holds a calculation called '<name>' (step '<key>'); … cannot be instantiated here twice, or beside a calculation of that name. Rename the existing one first.`

The pack templates are built in and carry example inputs. Every example input is recorded with the source `assumption: example input — replace with your own`, so the packs can be checked. The values are the repository's invented examples (see each pack's README under `docs\worked-examples\packs\`). They are not designs.

The generic steps are the same for all five. Each script lists the names, texts and numbers that differ.

- **Create.** Pick the pack template in **Pack template**, type the **Pack title**, press **Create pack from template**. Status reads `Pack '<title>' created from '<template>' with <n> linked calculation(s). Open any of them from the list, then check and issue the pack.` The pack is selected in **Packs in this project** as `<title> · rev A · Draft · <n> calculation(s)`. **Calculations in the selected pack** lists the members as `<name> · <template title> · rev A · Draft`. The suggested issue reference is `<title with spaces as hyphens>-A` and the client is the project name.
- **Look at a member.** Pick it in **Calculations in the selected pack** and press **Open pack member**. Status reads `Opened '<name>' · rev A · Draft.` A linked input shows source `link`. The *Link* panel and the banner are described in T-L1.
- **Break an upstream input.** Open the member named in the script, change the input, press **Save changes** (`Saved.`).
- **See STALE.** The Saved calculations list and the members list show ` · STALE` in amber on every step that depends on the change, directly or through another step. Open one: the orange `Stale — …` banner gives the reasons and **Update linked inputs** appears.
- **Check pack while stale.** Set **Checker's organisation** `Tempest Engineering` and **Check statement** `Checked in the worksheet form.` (the form suggests both), leave the outcome on *Accepted*, press **Check pack**. It is refused with the text in the script. **Issue pack** stays greyed out while the pack is a draft.
- **Update in order.** Open the first stale step, press **Update linked inputs** (`Linked inputs updated to the current revisions and recalculated.`). Press **Check pack** again. The refusal now names the next step that is still stale. A step that depended only on a step you just updated clears by itself and needs no press. Repeat until **Check pack** succeeds.
- **Check.** Status reads `Pack checked by <signed-in person>. Issue it when ready.` The pack shows `Checked`. **Reopen pack** becomes available (see T-C1).
- **Issue.** Check that **Issue reference** and **Client** are filled, press **Issue pack**. Status reads `Pack issued as '<reference>'. Its sheet and every calculation are attached to the pack's Evidence.` Open a member: the watermark reads ISSUED and the form is read-only.

### T-C1 Drive train

**Chain.** E1 motor sizing → M4 shaft → M5 bearing life → M2 seat fit; E1 → M3 coupling flange bolts. Pack template **Drive train** (`pack.drive-train`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\drive-train\README.md`.

| Member | Template | Checks when created |
|---|---|---|
| Motor sizing | Motor and actuator sizing | 3 PASS |
| Shaft | Shaft: static, fatigue, keys, critical speed | 4 PASS |
| Bearing A life | Rolling bearing life (ISO 281) | 2 PASS |
| Bearing A seat fit | ISO 286 limits and fits | 1 PASS |
| Coupling flange bolts | Bolt group (elastic method) | 4 PASS |

**Values that flow down each link.** The *Before* column is what the pack shows when created. The *After* column is what it shows once you have broken the upstream input and updated every step.

| Link | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Motor sizing `T_rms` → Shaft `T` | 0.151579 kN·m | 0.155789 kN·m |
| Motor sizing `n_pk` → Shaft `n` | 1500 rpm | 1500 rpm |
| Shaft `R_A` → Bearing A life `F_r` | 1.34604 kN | 1.34604 kN |
| Motor sizing `n_pk` → Bearing A life `n` | 1500 rpm | 1500 rpm |
| Shaft `d_A` → Bearing A seat fit `D` | 35 mm | 35 mm |
| Motor sizing `T_pk` → Shaft `T_pk` | 0.159378 kN·m | 0.163588 kN·m |
| Motor sizing `T_pk` → Coupling flange bolts `T_z` | 0.159378 kN·m | 0.163588 kN·m |

Open any of them from the list, then check and issue the pack.` **Packs in this project** shows `Conveyor head drive · rev A · Draft · 5 calculation(s)`. **Calculations in the selected pack** lists the 5 members (table above) as rev A, Draft, none STALE. | |

The checks are as listed. Note any member that opens blocked or stale. | |

Change `T_L` (constant load torque at the output) from 1800 N·m to 1850 N·m. **Save changes**. | Status `Saved.` `RMS torque SF·T_rms ≤ T_rated` moves from u 0.947 to 0.974, `Peak torque SF·T_pk ≤ T_peak` from 0.498 to 0.511. ### T-C2 Motor circuit (refused until you supply your own tables)

**Chain.** E1 motor sizing → E6 load budget → E4 cable and device → E3 voltage drop and E5 fault protection → E2 enclosure heat. Pack template **Motor circuit** (`pack.motor-circuit`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\motor-circuit\README.md`.

**Why this one is different.** The E4 step needs a cable-capacity (`ampacity`) table and a preferred device-rating (`ratings`) table. None ships with the program (§A5). The pack template carries example inputs for every step except those two tables, so **Create pack from template** is refused at the cable step and says so. The form's Create passes no inputs of its own, so you cannot supply the tables through it. Parts B and C show what you can do instead.

**Part A. The refusal (form).**

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open a new project. In **Calculation packs** pick **Motor circuit**, type the **Pack title** `Servo axis motor circuit`, press **Create pack from template**. | Status reads `Step 'e4' is blocked, so 'e3' cannot take 'I_b' from it: ampacity: enter the current-carrying capacities for the installation method from your own licensed table (record kind cable.ampacity). The cable step needs your own cable-capacity (ampacity) and preferred device-rating (ratings) tables: none ship with the program. Enter them from your copy of the wiring regulations and the device manufacturer's data, then create the pack again.` | |
| 2 | Look at **Saved calculations** and **Packs in this project**. | Both stay empty. The chain is run in memory first, so a refusal leaves no half-made calculations (`PackDryRunTests`). | |

**Part B. Automated proofs.**

```powershell
dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~MotorCircuitChainTests|FullyQualifiedName~PackExampleInputsTests"
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | Run the command. | Pass (6 + 8 tests). They cover: the demand reaching cable, voltage drop, fault check and enclosure; the fault check **blocked** when its device differs from the one the cable step sized (`device_code`); a bigger motor staling the chain and the update carrying the current through; the refusal at the cable step when the tables are missing; the chain created when only the cable step is given its tables; and the issued pack never exporting the licensed ampacity table. | |

**Part C. Build the chain yourself with your own tables (owner-led).** The form cannot make a pack from saved calculations, so this part stops at linked, saved calculations and the stale marker. Create the six calculations by hand, taking the inputs of each step from `docs\worked-examples\packs\motor-circuit\inputs.json` (one JSON object keyed by step; copy one step's list out of it and import it. Its links name the original calculations and do not resolve here, so re-make the links with the *Link* row). Give E4 your own `ampacity` and `ratings` tables and your own `C_a`, `C_g`, `C_i`. Use the **Save inputs as record** panel as in T-REC part 2 if you want the tables licensed.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | Make the links, upstream first, with the *Link* row: E1 `P_rms` → E6 `P_add`; E6 `I_3ph` → E4 `I_b_in`; E4 `I_b` → E3 `I`; E4 `L` → E3 `L`; E4 `CSA` → E3 `A_c`; E4 `CSA` → E5 `S_line`; E4 `L` → E5 `L`; E4 `I_n` → E5 `I_n`; E4 `device_code` → E5 `device_code`; E6 `Phi` → E2 `P_add`. | Each press reads `Linked <input> to <output>; it is pinned to that calculation's current revision.` A refusal for a dimension mismatch reads as in T-L1 step A5. The shipped example carries E1 `P_rms` 0.784142 kW into E6 and E6 `Phi` 140 W into E2 (README); your E4 results depend on your tables, so compare them with your own hand calculation (section 6). | |
| 5 | In E5 pick a protective device that is not the one E4 sized (for example type B when E4 chose type C). | E5’s governing bar reads `Blocked — device: the cable step sized the circuit for MCB type C (I_a = 10 I_n), but the device chosen here is MCB type B (I_a = 5 I_n); choose MCB type C (I_a = 10 I_n) (or change the cable step) so the fault check uses the device that was sized.` Put it back to the sized device and the block clears. The wording of the two device names follows the device list; the structure of the text is the one in the code. | |
| 6 | Open E1, change the moved mass `m`, **Save changes**. | E6 shows ` · STALE`. E4, E3, E5 and E2 follow (E4 takes `I_b_in` from E6; E3 and E5 take from E4; E2 from E6). Updating in order (E6, then E4, then E3, E5 and E2) clears them. | |
| 7 | Record anything that does not behave like the other four chains. | Check and issue of each step is by the single-calculation buttons. E4 and E5 demand an independent check (T-IND). A draft upstream blocks a downstream check until the upstream is checked and issued. | |

### T-REC Reference records and the licence lock

Part 1 tests the mechanism on M5, because M5 does not demand an independent check, so you can check and issue alone. The numbers are the repository's test values, not the SKF figures; the lock does not care. Part 2 sets up the E4 records from your own copies.

**What the lock does (§A5, DA-23).** A value taken from a record marked *Licensed source* never leaves the program as a value:

- The CSV export (**Export inputs (CSV)**) writes the symbol and the record pin and no value: the reference reads `record:<32 hex digits>@<rev> · licensed value withheld`. A licensed table is written as its own row only, with no rows.
- A CSV or JSON re-import of a withheld scalar is **blocked** until the record is applied again. A withheld table comes back empty with a warning, and the sheet is blocked.
- An issued sheet, a pack sheet and the preview withhold the **rows** of a table pinned to a licensed record. They print the record pin, its citation and `rows withheld (licensed record); the values used appear in the working`. A scalar from a licensed record is the value used and stays on the sheet.
- The lock follows the provenance, not the numbers (DA-18): if you retype a licensed value with another source, it exports.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Open a project. Open **Rolling bearing life (ISO 281)**. Import the `skf-6206-pump` JSON (as in T-00 step 1). Name it `T-REC bearing`. **Save as calculation**. | `Imported 15 input(s).` then saved. A **Reference records** panel appears (it shows only for a saved draft whose template declares record kinds). | |
| R2 | Pick the kind `Bearing load ratings`. Fill Title `T-REC ratings`, Source title, **Edition and amendment**, **Table or clause**, **Licence holder** (any text; all five are required). Tick **Licensed source (never exported)**. Press **Save inputs as record**. | Status `Saved 'T-REC ratings' as a reference record (rev 1, licensed: its values never leave in an export) and applied it.` | |
| R3 | Pick the kind `Bearing X, Y, e factors`. Fill the same fields, **leave Licensed source unticked**. Press **Save inputs as record**. | Status `Saved '…' as a reference record (rev 1) and applied it.` | |
| R4 | Open the **Take values from** picker. | Two entries, each `<citation> · rev 1`. | |
| R5 | Press **Export inputs (CSV)**. Paste into Notepad. | Rows `C` and `C_0` have an empty value and the reference `record:<32 hex digits>@1 · licensed value withheld`. Rows `X_1`, `Y_1`, `X`, `Y`, `e_lim`, `X_0`, `Y_0` carry their values and cite the second record. Other rows (for example `F_r`) carry their values. | |
| R6 | With that CSV still on the clipboard press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. | Nothing is imported: `Nothing imported: Row <n>: C: this value was withheld on export (licensed record) and is not in the file; apply the record again, or enter the value from your own copy. Row <m>: C_0: this value was withheld on export (licensed record) and is not in the file; apply the record again, or enter the value from your own copy.` The form is unchanged. The same text, without the row prefix, comes from a JSON import. | |

| R10 | Run `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~RecordTests\|FullyQualifiedName~BlueRoundTests"` and `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Sheets --configuration Release --no-build`. | Pass. They include `Values_From_A_Licensed_Record_Never_Leave_Through_An_Export`, `Licensed_Values_Cannot_Be_Laundered_Into_An_Exportable_Record`, `Applying_A_Record_Pins_It_And_A_Later_Revision_Makes_The_Calculation_Stale`, `R_A_01_An_Issued_Sheet_Withholds_The_Rows_Of_A_Licensed_Table_And_Shows_The_Value_Used`, `R_A_01_An_Issued_Pack_Sheet_And_A_Preview_Withhold_The_Licensed_Rows_Too`, `B_A_05_A_Licensed_Table_Stays_Withheld_Through_The_CSV_Round_Trip_And_An_Unlicensed_One_Comes_Back` and the Sheets CSV and JSON withheld-value tests. | |
| R11 | Look for a way to export a licensed record itself from the Desktop. | There is none. `ExportRecordAsync` refuses a licensed record (`Record '<title>' holds values from a licensed source (<source>, <edition>); its values are never exported.`) and only tests call it. Record that no button exists. | |

**Part 2. E4 records from your own copies.** The JSON skeleton below holds placeholders only. **Replace each `<…>` with a value from your own copy.** This pack supplies none. Add one `Cells` object per row. Keep the units as shown.

```powershell
@'
[{"Symbol":"ratings","Rows":[{"Cells":{"I_n":{"Number":<rated current>,"Unit":"A"}}}]},{"Symbol":"ampacity","Rows":[{"Cells":{"CSA":{"Number":<mm2>,"Unit":"mm^2"},"I_t":{"Number":<amps>,"Unit":"A"}}}]}]
'@ | Set-Clipboard
```

### 3.B Addendum B: foundations and calculators

Run T-AUTO first. T-REF, T-W3 and T-FPD cover the foundations under every Addendum B calculator. The calculator scripts follow, discipline by discipline, then the two import workflows (T-W1, T-W2). **T-S8 is a draft**: S8 is not in this tree. The expected numbers come from the worked examples under `docs\worked-examples\` (each script names its folder) and from running the real calculators headless on those inputs laid over a new sheet's defaults, which is what the form does.

### T-REF — The shared kernels: the B6 gas law and the M1 tolerance-stack engine

Phase B-0 moved two pieces of logic into the Engine without changing a number. The B6 sealed-system vessel now calls a shared polytropic gas-law kernel (`GasLaw`, which F2 also uses), and M1 now calls a pure tolerance-stack engine (`ToleranceStackEngine`, which F3 and W2 also use). The B6 and M1 sheets, their goldens and their known-answer cases are unchanged. This script re-runs one worked example of each and compares it with the values in the example folder, which are the same values the Addendum A and base scripts use. The automated proofs are in T-AUTO below (Engine `GasLawTests` and `ToleranceStackEngineGuardTests`; Calculators `ToleranceStackEngineTests`, `SealedSystemTests` and `ToleranceStackTests`).

**Part A. B6 expansion vessel**

**Inputs from** worked example `heat-pump-retrofit-55-45` (`docs\worked-examples\building-services\bs.sealed-system\heat-pump-retrofit-55-45`) — Heat pump retrofit at 55/45 °C: existing radiators and expansion vessel. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A five-room heat-pump retrofit at 55/45 °C flow and return with a 12 L vessel. The example FAILS by design: the emitters are too small for the room heat at that mean temperature difference.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):


### T-W3 — Material library (W3): apply a material, pin its revision, see it go stale

**What W3 is.** A material is a reference record of kind `material.properties`. It holds E, G, ν, ρ, α, λ, f_y, f_u, the R_m(d) curve coefficients, permissible strains, secant-modulus points and friction pairs, and temperature tables read linearly (never extrapolated). Each value carries its own source, edition, page or table, licence class, who entered it, when, and an evidence SHA-256. A calculation pins the material's revision. **W3 has no form panel of its own.** The existing **Reference records** panel applies a material to any template that maps an input to a material property. The library ships four **indicative** seed materials: carbon steel (generic), austenitic stainless steel (generic), aluminium alloy (generic) and copper (generic). Each carries a few rounded textbook figures for a class of material (E, G, ν, ρ, α, λ). They are not grades of a standard and not catalogue values. A design takes its values from the grade's own data, entered as a material of your own.

Eight Addendum B templates map 23 inputs to a material property: M7 (G, E, ρ and the two R_m(d) coefficients), M8 (the secant-modulus points, the two permissible strains and ν), S7 (E, G, f_y), S9 (E, ν, ρ, f_y), B8 (E, α), B9 (λ), F1 (E, f_y) and F3 (α, ν). This script uses M7, F1 and S7.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open a project. Open **Helical springs: compression, extension, torsion** (M7). Read the bar. Type `S_e` 400 MPa. Name the sheet `W3 spring`. Press **Save as calculation**. | A new sheet opens on the declared defaults only: `G` 80 GPa, `E` 206 GPa, `rho` 7850 kg/m³. Before you type, the bar reads `Blocked — S_e: enter 'Endurance strength for the stress kind (shear; bending in torsion) at N_c cycles, alternating (no default: from the wire maker or your fatigue reference)' in MPa; it has no default value.` Notices include, for `G`, `E` and `rho` alike, `G: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record (Material properties); enter it as one so its source, edition and licence travel with it.` After the save the **Reference records** panel appears. | |
| 2 | Open the **Take values from** picker. | Four entries at least, each `<title> — Generic textbook values (indicative), generic, Generic values (indicative) · rev 1`: `Aluminium alloy (generic)`, `Austenitic stainless steel (generic)`, `Carbon steel (generic)` and `Copper (generic)`. | |
| 3 | Pick `Carbon steel (generic) …` and press **Apply record**. | Status `Record applied: those inputs now cite it, its edition and its revision.` `G` reads 81 GPa, `E` 210 GPa, `rho` 7850 kg/m³. Each cites `record:<32 hex digits>@1 · G · Rounded handbook figure for the material class; not from a standard or a catalogue`, with the revision `generic`. The three "not taken from a reference record" notices for `G`, `E` and `rho` are gone. `Rm_A` and `Rm_m` stay empty (the seed states no curve), and `Rm` is still yours. The calculation is not stale. | |
| 4 | Change `G` to 85 GPa by typing and press **Save changes**. | Either the source of `G` changes to *manual* and the notice for `G` returns (the value is then a hand entry), or the sheet reports that `G` no longer matches the material it cites. The service reports the second: `'G' no longer matches 'Carbon steel (generic)' revision 1, which it cites: the value was changed after the record was applied. Apply the record again, or enter the value by hand.` Record which one the form shows. | |
| 5 | Press **Apply record** on the carbon steel again. | `G` returns to 81 GPa and cites the record. | |
| 6 | Save a material of your own. Type `G` 79.3 GPa, `E` 206 GPa and `rho` 7850 kg/m³ on the sheet. In the records panel pick the kind `Material properties (G, E, rho, Rm_A, Rm_m)`. Fill Title `Wire certificate WC-1`, Source title `Test certificate`, **Edition and amendment** `rev A`, **Table or clause** `p. 2`, **Licence holder** `Test Wire Co`. Leave **Licensed source** unticked. Press **Save inputs as record**. | Status `Saved 'Wire certificate WC-1' as a reference record (rev 1) and applied it.` The three inputs now cite `record:<id>@1 · G · p. 2` with the revision `rev A`. The picker now lists `Wire certificate WC-1 — Test certificate, rev A, p. 2 · rev 1` after the four seeds. | |
| 7 | Open **Cylinder and actuator circuit** (F1), save a draft, pick `Carbon steel (generic)` and press **Apply record**. | `E` becomes 210 GPa and cites the record. `f_y` stays 500 MPa and still shows the "not taken from a reference record" notice: the seed states no yield strength, so it leaves `f_y` to you. | |
| 8 | Open **EN 1993-1-1 LTB and N+M interaction** (S7), save a draft, pick `Austenitic stainless steel (generic)` and press **Apply record**. | `E` 195 GPa and `G` 75 GPa cite the record. `f_y` stays 355 MPa with its notice. (The seed is for a first estimate. It is no grade under EN 10088.) | |
| 9 | Repeat step 6 with the title `Wire certificate WC-2` and **Licensed source (never exported)** ticked. Press **Export inputs (CSV)**. | The rows `G`, `E` and `rho` have an empty value and the reference `record:<32 hex digits>@1 · licensed value withheld`, as for any licensed record (T-REC R5). | |
| 10 | Automated proofs: `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~Materials"` and the `LicensedLinkTests`. | Pass (48 cases under `Materials/`, 8 in `LicensedLinkTests`). They include: the seed materials are generic, unlicensed and flagged indicative; no standard or catalogue table is seeded; a material round-trips with each value's source stamp and evidence hash; a later revision stales every calculation pinned to the earlier one; a temperature table is read linearly and refused outside its ends; one licensed value makes the whole material licensed; and licensed values never leave through a material export or an issued sheet. | |

**What to record.** The text the form shows in step 4. Whether any template of Addendum B takes a property it should not. The label of each seed in the picker (they are long).

### T-FPD — Fluid Power discipline (ADR-0170)

**What it is.** Fluid Power is the fifth calculation discipline and the sixth engineering domain. The Engineering tree and a project's Structure tab list it beside Structural, Electrical and Building Services. It holds three kinds of object, each with typed attributes: **Circuit** (circuit type, working pressure in bar, fluid, flow rate in L/min), **Actuator** (actuator type, bore, rod diameter and stroke in mm, working pressure in bar) and **Power unit** (pump type, displacement in cm³/rev, motor power in kW, reservoir volume in L, relief setting in bar). **The attributes are recorded as typed.** No rating, no safety factor and no design-code check applies to them. Pneumatics (ISO 6358) is out of scope and has no placeholder. The expected texts below come from `ADR-0170` and the Core test `DisciplineWorkspaceTests`. I did not run the Desktop windows, so the menu wording is to be confirmed.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|

| 2 | Create a Circuit named `Press circuit`. Set `Circuit type` `Open-centre, load sensing`, `Working pressure (bar)` `9999`, `Fluid` `ISO VG 46 mineral oil`. | Every value is accepted exactly as typed. 9999 bar raises no rating warning (by design). The inspector shows `Flow rate (L/min)` as `(not recorded)`. | |
| 3 | Try to set `Bore (mm)` on the circuit. | Refused. The message names the circuit's own attributes, including `Working pressure (bar)`. An attribute belongs to its own kind. | |
| 4 | Create an Actuator `Clamp cylinder` and a Power unit `Press pump`. Fill their attributes. Close the program and open it again on the same data folder. | The objects and their attributes survive the restart. | |
| 5 | Request review, approve and release the circuit (the same commands as the other disciplines, under the prefix `fluid-power`). | They follow the same lifecycle as a Structural member. To be confirmed. | |
| 6 | Open Engineering → Modules → Calculations → Worksheets, and look under Disciplines. | A **Fluid Power** node lists three worksheets: **Cylinder and actuator circuit** (F1), **Accumulator sizing (gas law)** (F2) and **O-ring and seal gland** (F3). Ctrl+K finds each by name, in the category Fluid Power. | |
| 7 | Run `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.FluidPower --configuration Release --no-build` and `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Core.Tests --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~DisciplineWorkspaceTests"`. | Pass (FluidPower 88 cases, of which `FluidPowerDisciplineTests` 8). The Core filter includes the Fluid Power attribute and restart tests. | |

### T-M7 — Helical springs: compression, extension, torsion (`mech.spring@1.2.0`)

M7 sits in the Mechanical discipline. This script covers the compression mode, the only mode that has a worked example. The extension and torsion modes have known-answer cases (`ka-extension`, `ka-torsion`) in the automated suite (`SpringTests`, T-AUTO below). R_m(d), the permissible stress fractions, the endurance strength and the seating coefficient have no default: they are your records (section 5.4). The example enters them as assumptions.

**Inputs from** worked example `plunger-return-spring` (`docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.spring\plunger-return-spring`) — Plunger return spring: squared and ground compression spring, 60 to 180 N. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 3 mm wire at 24 mm mean diameter with 9 active coils, squared and ground ends, working between 60 N and 180 N. The EN 13906-1 solid length (maximum wire diameter, minimum coil clearance) is selected, so the coil-clearance check governs.

### T-M8 — Snap-fits: cantilever, annular, torsion (`mech.snap-fit@1.3.0`)

M8 takes the friction pair, the secant modulus and the permissible strain from your records or from a W3 material (T-W3). The example types the secant modulus (`Es_source`) and states the permissible strain. The strain is the nominal surface strain at the critical section; the root fillet is not modelled (the sheet says so).

**Inputs from** worked example `enclosure-lid-hook` (`docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.snap-fit\enclosure-lid-hook`) — Enclosure lid hook: cantilever snap-fit, thickness tapered to half. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A tapered-thickness cantilever hook on an enclosure lid, 18 mm long, 2.2 mm thick at the root and 7 mm wide, deflected 1.2 mm, in a plastic with a 2000 MPa secant modulus.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.snap-fit\enclosure-lid-hook\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 14 inputs
```

The reference line starts `mech.snap-fit@1.3.0  ·  hash`. | |

### T-M9 — Mechanism kinematics: four-bar, slider-crank, cam (`mech.mechanism@1.2.0`)

M9 has two worked examples, a four-bar linkage and a cam. Run both. The sweep outputs (crank angle, coupler angle, torque and the like) are series of 73 values; the form shows them as "(73 items)". The loop-closure residual checks are self-consistency checks: they stay at about 1e-14 mm.

**Part A. Four-bar crank-rocker**

**Inputs from** worked example `carton-tucker-crank-rocker` (`docs\worked-examples\mechanical\mech.mechanism\carton-tucker-crank-rocker`) — Carton flap tucker: crank-rocker 150/45/140/100 mm at 60 cycles/min. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 150 / 45 / 140 / 100 mm four-bar (ground, crank, coupler, rocker) turned at 60 rpm against a 4 N·m load that opposes the motion. The crank is the shortest link, so it is a Grashof crank-rocker.

### T-E7 — PCB trace width (IPC-2221 legacy) (`elec.pcb-trace@1.0.1`)

E7 is the legacy IPC-2221 curve-fit. The sheet labels it "legacy IPC-2221 curve-fit, conservative for multilayer boards". The constants (`k_ext`, `k_int`, the two exponents, the copper thickness per ounce) are published closed-form constants in a method record; the sheet warns until you apply one (section 5.4).

### T-E9 — Motor start voltage dip and PF correction (`elec.motor-start@1.2.0`)

E9 gives the voltage dip at the motor terminals and at the point of common coupling when a motor starts, and the capacitor kvar to a target power factor. It is a screening: the dip is a lower bound when only the transformer terminals are modelled. The two examples leave seven inputs at their template defaults (fault level basis and the soft-starter and drive data), so the sheet warns about each.

**Part A. Direct-on-line start from a 500 kVA transformer**

**Inputs from** worked example `pump-dol-500kva` (`docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.motor-start\pump-dol-500kva`) — 75 kW pump motor started direct on line from a 500 kVA transformer. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 75 kW pump motor on 40 m of 50 mm² cable, started direct on line, supplied by a 500 kVA transformer with 4 % impedance.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\electrical\elec.motor-start\pump-dol-500kva\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 23 inputs
```

|---|---|---|---|

Pick **Motor start voltage dip and PF correction** in the catalogue (or press Ctrl+K and type its name). | The right pane heading reads **Motor start voltage dip and PF correction**. The reference line starts `elec.motor-start@1.2.0  ·  hash`. | |

### T-S7 — EN 1993-1-1 LTB and N+M interaction (`struct.ltb-interaction@1.1.0`)

S7 checks lateral-torsional buckling, flexural buckling and the N+M interaction of a doubly-symmetric member between fork supports. The edition switch (2005+A1:2014 or 2022) changes the printed label only: the arithmetic is that of 2005+A1:2014, and the sheet says so. The Annex A and Annex B coefficients are typed from the published expressions and are **Unconfirmed** (section 4.5, U-14 to U-17, and the worked example in section 4.7): check them against your copy before you rely on the interaction factors. The imperfection factors, `C_1`, `C_2` and `k_c` are your Eurocode National Annex records (section 5.4); the example enters them as assumptions. The section properties are the S1 section sheet's for a UB 457×191×98; S1 and S2 can supply them by link in your own work.

**Inputs from** worked example `ub-457x191x98-beam-column` (`docs\worked-examples\structural\struct.ltb-interaction\ub-457x191x98-beam-column`) — Beam-column UB 457×191×98 S355, 5 m between restraints: LTB, flexural buckling and N+M interaction. Status: Unconfirmed (analytic; the Annex B coefficients are to be checked; see section 4.5).

A beam-column between restraints 5 m apart carrying 400 kN compression with end moments of 340 and 170 kN·m in single curvature and a 6 kN·m minor-axis moment. Equation 6.62 of the interaction governs.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

### T-S8 — Lifting lugs and spreader/lifting beams (`struct.lifting`, pending B-5)

**To be confirmed when B-5 merges.** S8 is **not in this tree**. It is being finished on another branch (`v0.26.0/addB-5-gated`), behind the feature flag `Calc:Features:Lifting`, which is **off by default**. I wrote this script from the B-5 brief (`brief-B5-S8.md`) and §B6 of the addendum. No `docs/calcs` page for S8 exists at `5a8ba277`, and no template id, version, input symbol or output symbol of S8 has been seen in code. Every *to be confirmed* below is a place to fill in. **Do not enable the flag on any machine that issues real calculations before section 10 (the S8 review checklist) is done.**

**The scope S8 will have (from the brief).** BTH-1 mode (clause 3-3.3 of ASME BTH-1 is the one clause Addendum B names): a dishing correction C_r that depends on the ratio of pin to hole diameter, tension beyond the hole, single-plane fracture, double-plane shear at an angle φ that depends on the same ratio, bearing and a dishing ratio. The expressions are in Addendum B §B6 S8. N_d comes from a user record by Design Category. EN 13155 mode: an elastic load case and a no-release load case up to a stated number of cycles, with the factors that §B6 S8 gives, the calculation route of EN 13001-3-1 cited, and an automatic tilt load case for horizontal beams. A third mode runs both. Inputs: WLL, sling angle, out-of-plane angle, t, Dh, Dp, R, be, a, F_y and F_u (from a W3 material), Design Category or Service Class, and mode. Outputs: a capacity and a utilisation for each mode, the governing mode, a proof-load value and a marking and certification checklist (text). Ports: `lug.capacity`, `lug.governing_mode`, `spreader.util`. **No standards text or table ships.** N_d by Design Category, the dishing-ratio limits, the service-class factors and every coefficient table are your records.

**Governance (§B5).** The sheet prints "design calculation, not a thorough examination" and the LOLER and PUWER scope notes. Issue needs a competent-person attestation (name, qualification, statement; required, not empty, not the creator) and a **second** Checked signature (two different checkers, neither the creator nor an editor). The attestation and the second signature are **recorded names only**. Nothing authenticates them. Anyone who can type can type any name. The checklist in section 10 asks you to decide whether that is enough.

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Flag off (the default).** Open the worksheets. Search the catalogue for `lifting`. Press Ctrl+K and type `lifting lug`. | S8 is not listed anywhere: not in the Worksheets tree, not under Structural, not in Ctrl+K. To be confirmed. | |
| 2 | With the flag off, try every other way in. Import a CSV or JSON for template `struct.lifting`. Create a pack that holds it. Open a calculation of that template saved before (a data folder from a build with the flag on). | Each is refused with a clear message. An existing instance cannot be issued. To be confirmed (the brief lists these bypass paths). | |
| 3 | Look for the flag. Find `appsettings.json` next to `Tempest.Desktop.exe` (for `dotnet run`, in the build output folder of the Desktop project). | The file holds no `Lifting` setting by default. To be confirmed. | |
| 4 | Try to turn the flag on from somewhere else. Set the environment variable `Calc__Features__Lifting` to `true` and start the app. Try a command-line switch. | S8 stays hidden. **The flag is read only from `appsettings.json` next to the executable.** To be confirmed. Record what you see; a flag that an environment variable can set is a finding. | |
| 5 | Enable it. Edit `appsettings.json`: `"Calc": { "Features": { "Lifting": true } }`. Close every instance (one instance per data folder). Start the app. | **Lifting lugs and spreader/lifting beams** (name to be confirmed) is listed under Structural, and Ctrl+K finds it. | |
| 6 | Open the sheet and read the bar before you type. | The default state is to be confirmed (a blocked sheet saying what to enter, or a reading of defaults). The sheet names the mode. | |
| 7 | Enter the PDHonline S106 lug in BTH-1 mode (the addendum's second-source case): Cr = 0.78, φ = 33°, shear area Av = 2.964 in², double-plane shear 19.849 kip, single-plane fracture 20.539 kip, bearing 10.875 kip. Enter the geometry and the design factor from the source (you hold it; the case sets the harness factor to match the source's allowable-stress basis). Pick the N_d from your own record. | The results match the six values to the case's tolerance, after unit conversion (1 kip = 4.4482 kN; 1 in² = 645.16 mm²). The case is tagged **Secondary** (a course-notes source) and is expected to stay Unconfirmed until you check it (section 4.5). To be confirmed. | |

| 9 | Try **Issue** without an attestation. | Refused, naming the missing attestation. To be confirmed. | |
| 10 | Sign in as the author. Fill the attestation with the author's own name. Press **Issue**. | Refused: the attestation cannot come from the creator. To be confirmed. | |
| 11 | Have a second person record the first check. Press **Issue** with only one Checked signature. | Refused: a second, different checker is needed. To be confirmed. | |
| 12 | Have a third person record the second check. Press **Issue**. | Issued. The sheet prints "design calculation, not a thorough examination" and the scope notes. Open the PDF and record the exact wording. To be confirmed. | |
| 13 | Type a name that is not a person (`Nobody`) into the attestation. | It is accepted as typed, if the form behaves as the brief says. **Record this.** Nothing authenticates the attestation or the second signature. | |
| 14 | Turn the flag off again and restart. Open the issued S8 calculation. | Whether an issued sheet stays readable with the flag off is to be confirmed. It cannot be revised. | |

### T-S9 — Plate bending (`struct.plate-bending@1.2.1`)

S9 covers circular and rectangular plates with uniform, patch and ring loads. The worked example is a simply supported rectangle under uniform pressure (Navier series). The known-answer cases that compare with the classical coefficients for square plates are **Unconfirmed** (section 4.5, U-26 to U-29). The example leaves the patch and ring inputs at their defaults, so the sheet warns about each.

**Inputs from** worked example `enclosure-lid-aluminium` (`docs\worked-examples\structural\struct.plate-bending\enclosure-lid-aluminium`) — Aluminium enclosure lid 300 × 200 × 4 mm, simply supported, 20 kPa internal pressure rise. ### T-B7 — Glaser condensation (interstitial and surface) (`bs.glaser@1.2.0`)

B7 is the Glaser condensation method over twelve months. The monthly climate, the surface resistances, the critical humidity and the condensate limit are your records (section 5.4). **The climate in both examples is invented** (a maritime year); it is not ISO 13788 data. The sheet warns that EN 15026 is the better tool for timber and breathable construction.

**Part A. Internal insulation without a vapour control layer**

**Inputs from** worked example `internal-insulation-no-vcl` (`docs\worked-examples\building-services\bs.glaser\internal-insulation-no-vcl`) — Solid brick wall with internal mineral wool and no vapour control layer: interstitial condensation over a year. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 12.5 mm plasterboard, 50 mm mineral wool and 215 mm brick wall at 20 °C and 50 % inside. Condensation starts in month 11 and peaks in month 3.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\building-services\bs.glaser\internal-insulation-no-vcl\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 11 inputs
```

### T-B8 — Pipe expansion, loops and anchor forces (screening) (`bs.pipe-expansion@1.2.0`)

B8 is a screening for pipe expansion: movement, restrained force, the leg or loop that absorbs it, the anchor force and moment, and the OD·Y/(L−U)² ratio against a limit K1. The sheet says it is a screening only; a line that fails it needs a formal stress analysis. Sa and K1 are your records (section 5.4). The examples' values (Sa 60 and 100 MPa, K1 100 and 120 mm²/m²) are invented. Run all three examples.

**Part A. U-loop on a low-temperature hot-water main**

**Inputs from** worked example `lthw-main-expansion-loop` (`docs\worked-examples\building-services\bs.pipe-expansion\lthw-main-expansion-loop`) — Low-temperature hot-water main, 50 m between anchors: a U-loop of two 4 m legs. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 50 m steel main, 88.9 × 3.2 mm, from 10 °C to 80 °C, with a U-loop whose legs are 4 m.
