### T-B9 — Insulation thickness (ISO 12241 formula route) (`bs.insulation@1.1.0`)

B9 is the ISO 12241 formula route: the thickness that meets a heat-loss limit, a surface-temperature limit and an anti-condensation margin, solved for the outer diameter, and the heat flow and surface temperature at the thickness you choose. The heat-flow targets and the dew-point margin are your records (BS 5422 targets, section 5.4). The surface coefficient is either typed or taken from a convection and radiation correlation. Run both examples.

**Part A. Chilled-water pipe, anti-condensation**

**Inputs from** worked example `chilled-water-pipe-condensation` (`docs\worked-examples\building-services\bs.insulation\chilled-water-pipe-condensation`) — Chilled-water pipe in a plant room: heat-gain limit and anti-condensation, 25 mm chosen. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 90 mm pipe carrying 6 °C water in 28 °C, 70 % air, with 25 mm of λ 0.035 insulation and a 12 W/m heat-gain limit.

### T-F1 — Cylinder and actuator circuit (`fp.cylinder@1.3.0`)

F1 is the first Fluid Power worksheet (the discipline is new: ADR-0170). It gives the cylinder areas and forces, speeds, cycle time, the pump flow and power, the line velocities against your limits, the recommended bore, the orifice flow and a rod-buckling screening. The sheet labels the buckling check "screening only; ISO/TS 13725 method not implemented". Line velocity limits, the hose size list, the mounting factor and the buckling safety factor are your records (section 5.4). F1 is one of four calculators with a hard FAIL outside its valid range (S7, S8, F1, F2).

**Inputs from** worked example `press-ram-125-90-pump-and-lines` (`docs\worked-examples\fluid-power\fp.cylinder\press-ram-125-90-pump-and-lines`) — Press ram: 125/90 cylinder at 180 bar on a 45 cm³/rev pump, lines checked, rod screened for buckling. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 125 / 90 mm × 800 mm press ram at 180 bar, fed by a 45 cm³/rev pump at 1450 rpm (60 L/min circuit flow), pushing 150 kN and pulling 60 kN. The hose list is 16 to 63 mm. The pressure line is judged at the retract discharge, the larger stroke flow.

### T-F2 — Accumulator sizing (gas law) (`fp.accumulator@1.2.0`)

F2 sizes a gas-charged accumulator with the shared gas-law kernel (T-REF). It works in absolute pressure. It shows the bar·litre value and whether it is below the PSSR Schedule 1 250 bar·L exception; it is an indication only and never a pressure-vessel code check. The pre-charge rules and the size list are your records (section 5.4). F2 is a hard FAIL outside its valid range.

**Inputs from** worked example `clamp-circuit-bladder-accumulator` (`docs\worked-examples\fluid-power\fp.accumulator\clamp-circuit-bladder-accumulator`) — Bladder accumulator for a clamp circuit: 14 L delivered between 100 and 210 bar. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A 14 L emergency stroke between 210 bar and 100 bar with a 90 bar pre-charge, n = 1, 30 to 50 °C, charged at 20 °C, in a 230 bar vessel, from a 10 / 20 / 32 / 50 L size list. The pressures are absolute. The sheet warns that nitrogen departs from the ideal gas above 200 bar.

### T-F3 — O-ring and seal gland (`fp.oring-gland@1.2.0`)

F3 checks an O-ring gland: squeeze, volume-based fill, installed stretch over temperature, extrusion gap, each as worst case, RSS and a Monte Carlo (20 000 samples, seed 42). It uses the shared tolerance-stack engine (T-REF) and accepts measured groove distributions from W2 (T-W2, T-C7). The ring size and tolerance, the squeeze/fill/stretch bands and the permitted extrusion gap are your records (AS568 / ISO 3601 sizes, Parker limits; section 5.4). The sheet warns until you enter a permitted gap. No AS568, ISO 3601 or Parker chart data ships.

**Inputs from** worked example `locking-cylinder-piston-seal` (`docs\worked-examples\fluid-power\fp.oring-gland\locking-cylinder-piston-seal`) — Static piston O-ring seal of a 63 mm locking cylinder: squeeze, fill, stretch and extrusion over tolerance, temperature and swell. Status: Confirmed (analytic; recomputed in python3 in the repository).

A static piston seal: 63 mm bore, 57.5 mm groove root, 3.5 mm cross-section on a 56 mm ring, 4.7 mm groove width, −25 to 70 °C, 160 bar, 70 Shore A, normal distributions with k = 3.

The worked example carries a source on every input. Copy its inputs to the clipboard from PowerShell, run from the repository root, then press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (step 3):

```powershell
Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\fluid-power\fp.oring-gland\locking-cylinder-piston-seal\inputs.json | Set-Clipboard   # 39 inputs
```

### T-W1 — CAD parameter import (W1)

W1 reads parameters out of a CAD file into the inputs of a draft calculation. It is a workflow with no template and no sheet of its own: its marks on a sheet are the **source of each imported input** and the **import record** on the calculation. Files are untrusted: the reader caps a file at 5 MB, refuses control characters and macros, and **never evaluates an expression** (a right-hand side with a reference, a function or an operator stays read-only text). This script uses an invented NX expression file and a mapping profile into M1 (`mech.tolerance-stack`); the same panel reads the Creo, SolidWorks, Onshape and generic formats (golden files for each are under `tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Import/Samples/`). The format layouts are **Unconfirmed**: the samples are invented from each format's documented shape (section 4.8). The panel needs a **saved, unedited draft**. Chain 3 (T-C8) adds a linked M2 fit.

Create the files. The profile text is what the product writes for the profile in `CadDrivenToleranceChainTests`:

```powershell
$w = "$HOME\TempestOS-Test-Data\w1"; New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $w | Out-Null
@'
// Expression file created by NX 2306 (invented)
// Part: STACK-1
// Revision: A
[mm]plate_len=30
[mm]spacer_len=10
[mm]pin_len=5
[mm]gap_min=24.5
[mm]gap_max=25.5
[mm]derived=plate_len-spacer_len
[mm]flange=40 // not in the profile
'@ | Set-Content -Encoding utf8 "$w\stack-1.exp"
@'
{"id":"nx-stack","version":1,"title":"NX stack lengths to M1","entries":[{"cadName":"plate_len","target":"contributors[0].nominal","required":false},{"cadName":"spacer_len","target":"contributors[1].nominal","required":false},{"cadName":"pin_len","target":"contributors[2].nominal","required":false},{"cadName":"gap_min","target":"minimum","required":false},{"cadName":"gap_max","target":"maximum","required":false}],"system":"nx","templateId":"mech.tolerance-stack","ignoreCase":false}
'@ | Set-Content -Encoding utf8 "$w\nx-stack.json"
```

The M1 draft the import fills (a three-part pin stack that the file overwrites):

```powershell
@'
[{"Symbol":"contributors","Rows":[{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Plate"},"direction":{"Text":"adds"},"nominal":{"Number":29,"Unit":"mm"},"upper":{"Number":0.1,"Unit":"mm"},"lower":{"Number":-0.1,"Unit":"mm"}}},{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Spacer"},"direction":{"Text":"subtracts"},"nominal":{"Number":9,"Unit":"mm"},"upper":{"Number":0.05,"Unit":"mm"},"lower":{"Number":-0.05,"Unit":"mm"}}},{"Cells":{"name":{"Text":"Pin"},"direction":{"Text":"adds"},"nominal":{"Number":5,"Unit":"mm"},"upper":{"Number":0.02,"Unit":"mm"},"lower":{"Number":-0.02,"Unit":"mm"}}}]},{"Symbol":"minimum","Number":20,"Unit":"mm"},{"Symbol":"maximum","Number":30,"Unit":"mm"},{"Symbol":"k","Number":3},{"Symbol":"f_rss","Number":1.5},{"Symbol":"cpk_target","Number":1.33},{"Symbol":"n_mc","Number":2000},{"Symbol":"seed","Number":1},{"Symbol":"notation","Text":"BS 8888"}]
'@ | Set-Clipboard
```

### T-W2 — Scan deviation import to statistics, Cpk and M1 (W2)

W2 reads an inspection table (ZEISS or GOM Inspect, multi-stage, PolyWorks or Geomagic text) through **your own column profile**, computes the mean, σ, Cp, Cpk, Pp and Ppk of each feature over N parts with an Anderson–Darling normality check, and pushes a fitted **normal** into M1 (a stack contributor), F3 (a measured-dimension row) or a single input. It is a workflow with no template. The statistics are never trimmed silently; outliers are reported and kept unless you tick the box. Fewer than 2 parts: not computable. Fewer than 8: normality is not assessed. Fewer than 30 (adjustable): a capability warning. **The PDF table path is not built**: export the table as CSV or `.xlsx`. The header spellings of real exports are **Unconfirmed** (section 4.8); the profile is where you say what your export calls each column.

The table is invented: ten parts of a groove root diameter (nominal 51.5 mm, ±0.05) and one bore row that the profile does not map. The profile JSON is what the product writes.

### 3.D The ten §B7 chains: create, follow the values, break an upstream input, mend it, check, issue

Chains 1, 4, 5 and 7 to 10 are built-in pack templates (T-C6, T-C9, T-C10, T-C12 to T-C15). Chains 2 and 3 are workflows through the import panels (T-C7, T-C8). Chain 6 waits for S8 (T-C11). The generic steps are those of section 3.C: create, open a member, break an upstream input, see STALE, check while stale, update in order, check, issue. **Use a new project for each chain script**, for the reason section 3.C gives. T-C6 is different: the form refuses to create that pack until you supply your cable tables, as it does for the motor circuit (T-C2). The pack READMEs are under `docs\worked-examples\packs\`. Their values are invented and are not designs.

### T-C6 Hydraulic power unit (chain 1)

**Chain.** Cylinder and pump circuit (`fp.cylinder`) → Emergency stroke accumulator, Pressure line, Pump drive motor; Pump drive motor (`elec.motor-sizing`) → Motor supply cable, Pump motor start; Motor supply cable (`elec.conductor-sizing`) → Motor supply voltage drop, Motor supply fault protection, Pump motor start; Motor supply fault protection (`elec.fault-protection`) → Pump motor start. Pack template **Hydraulic power unit** (`pack.hydraulic-power-unit`), 8 steps and 20 links. README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\hydraulic-power-unit\README.md`. Section 11 turns this script into a run on one of your own jobs.

**Why this one needs your tables.** Like the motor circuit (T-C2), this pack holds a cable step (E4) that needs a cable-capacity (`ampacity`) table and a preferred device-rating (`ratings`) table. None ships (§A5). Three later steps take a value from the cable step (the voltage drop, the fault protection and the motor start), so **Create pack from template** is refused at the cable step and creates nothing. This differs from T-C9, where nothing takes from the cable step and the pack is created with that step blocked. The form's Create passes no inputs of its own, so you cannot give the pack your tables through it. Part A shows the refusal. Part B names the automated proofs. Part C builds the chain by hand, calculation by calculation, which is how you run it on a real job.

**The example is invented.** A 100/70 × 500 mm cylinder at 160 bar, a 28 cm³/rev pump at 1450 rpm, a 12 m line, a candidate motor of 120 N·m at 1460 rpm (an invented nameplate), a 400 kVA, 4 % transformer (invented) and a 30 m cable. The README warns that the cable tables of the example are synthetic test values and that the example must not size a real cable.

**Part A. The refusal (form).**

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Open a new project. In **Calculation packs** pick **Hydraulic power unit**, type the **Pack title** `Press HPU`, press **Create pack from template**. | Status reads `Step 'e4' is blocked, so 'e3' cannot take 'I_b' from it: ampacity: enter the current-carrying capacities for the installation method from your own licensed table (record kind cable.ampacity). The cable step needs your own cable-capacity (ampacity) and preferred device-rating (ratings) tables: none ship with the program. Enter them from your copy of the wiring regulations and the device manufacturer's data, then create the pack again.` The first step that takes from the cable step is the voltage drop, so the text names `e3`. | |
| 2 | Look at **Saved calculations** and **Packs in this project**. | Both stay empty. The chain runs in memory first, so a refusal leaves no half-made calculations (`PackDryRunTests`). | |

**Part B. Automated proofs.**

```powershell
dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~HydraulicPowerUnitChainTests|FullyQualifiedName~PackExampleInputsTests|FullyQualifiedName~ChainPackWorkedExampleTests"
```

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | Run the command. | Pass (12 + 13 + 12 tests). They cover: the values and units on every link; that each binding is a real link from the output it names; a bigger cylinder and pump staling every step below, refusing the check and the issue, and the update in order carrying the values through; a re-bored cylinder line staling the pressure line and the update carrying the bore into its segment table (the one table-cell link of the chain); the stale refusal naming the stale step; check and issue as one bundle once the cable step has an independent check; the refusal at the cable step without your tables; the refusal naming `n_rated` when the motor's rated speed is missing (the cable is sized on the rated power); the chain created when only the cable step is given its tables; a smaller supply transformer staling only the motor start; a different voltage factor on the fault protection reaching the motor start without a spurious warning; and the issue staleness gate when the cylinder pressure moves on. | |

**Part C. Build the chain yourself (owner-led).** The form cannot make a pack from saved calculations, so this part stops at linked, saved calculations and the stale marker. It makes **19 of the 20 links**. The twentieth, the cylinder's line bore `d_prs` into the first row of the pressure line's segment table, is a table-cell link, and the *Link* row of the form makes no cell link. Type that bore (20 mm) into the segment table instead. The automated test in Part B covers the cell link.

Copy each step's input list out of the issued record. The record is one JSON object keyed by step (`f1`, `f2`, `b1`, `e1`, `e4`, `e3`, `e5`, `e9`). In PowerShell, from the repository root:

```powershell
$hpu = Get-Content -Raw docs\worked-examples\packs\hydraulic-power-unit\inputs.json | ConvertFrom-Json
$hpu.f1 | ConvertTo-Json -Depth 10 | Set-Clipboard    # F1: 30 inputs. Change f1 to f2 (14), b1 (14), e1 (26), e4 (20), e3 (15), e5 (22) or e9 (30).
```

If `ConvertTo-Json` flattens a table in your PowerShell version, open `inputs.json` in Notepad and copy the step's array by hand. The links in the record name the original calculations and do not resolve in your project. Make them again with the *Link* row, upstream first. **The cable step's list holds the repository's synthetic `ampacity` and `ratings` tables** (invented values, labelled as such). Use them only to reproduce the numbers below. For a real job, replace both tables with your own (T-REC part 2) before you trust the cable.

### T-C8 CAD-driven tolerance (chain 3)

**Chain.** NX expression file (W1) → Pin stack (`mech.tolerance-stack`) → Seat fit (`mech.iso286-fit`). This is the whole of T-W1 steps 1 to 12, run as the chain: the import fills M1, the fit takes M1's nominal result by a link, and a re-import of a changed file makes the fit stale. The §B7 line also names M8; the repository test links M2 only. It is **not a pack template**.

| # | Check | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Run T-W1 steps 1 to 3. | The fit takes `D` = 25 mm from `x_nom` of the stack. | |
| 2 | T-W1 steps 4 to 8. | The first import leaves `Seat fit` fresh: `x_nom` stays 25 mm. | |
| 3 | T-W1 steps 10 and 11. | The re-import with `plate_len` 31 mm moves `x_nom` to 26 mm. | |
| 4 | T-W1 step 12. | `Seat fit` is STALE, its banner names `x_nom`, **Update linked inputs** gives `D` = 26 mm. Record check on `Seat fit` is refused while it is stale. | |
| 5 | Automated: `dotnet test tests/Tempest.Calc.Tests.Library --configuration Release --no-build --filter "FullyQualifiedName~CadDrivenToleranceChainTests"`. | 10 cases pass. They cover: an NX file driving the stack through a stored profile with provenance, the diff of a changed file, an unchanged file leaving the fit fresh, a refused import changing nothing, a checked or issued calculation never changed in place, and the import record surviving a store round trip. | |

### T-C9 Mechanism review (chain 4)

**Chain.** Tamper cam (`mech.mechanism`) → Follower return spring, Cam shaft, Cam shaft bearing A, Cam drive motor; Cam shaft (`mech.shaft`) → Cam shaft bearing A; Cam drive motor (`elec.motor-sizing`) → Cam drive supply cable. Pack template **Mechanism review** (`pack.mechanism-review`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\mechanism-review\README.md`.

**Why this one needs your tables.** The cable step (E4) needs a cable-capacity (`ampacity`) table and a preferred device-rating (`ratings`) table. None ships (§A5). Nothing takes from the cable step, so unlike the motor circuit the pack **is created**, and the cable step opens blocked. The pack cannot be checked until you give E4 your own tables (the rows after the last update). The cable step's figures in this script come from the repository's run with its synthetic test tables; compare yours with a hand calculation (section 6). The cam's RMS torque enters the shaft as its steady torque `T`, a stand-in the README explains: M4 models the torque as steady, while the cam's is fully alternating. The spring's rate and preload are typed in the cam step and are not linked back (that would be a cycle); the spring step warns `travel-mismatch` if its own working travel departs from the cam's lift by more than 1 %.

| Member | Template | Checks when created |
|---|---|---|
| Tamper cam | Mechanism kinematics: four-bar, slider-crank, cam | 3 PASS |
| Follower return spring | Helical springs: compression, extension, torsion | 4 PASS |
| Cam shaft | Shaft: static, fatigue, keys, critical speed | 4 PASS |
| Cam shaft bearing A | Rolling bearing life (ISO 281) | 2 PASS |
| Cam drive motor | Motor and actuator sizing | 3 PASS |
| Cam drive supply cable | Conductor sizing and overload coordination | blocked until you enter your tables (3 PASS in the repository run, which uses synthetic test tables) |

**Values that flow down each link.** The *Before* column is what the pack shows when created. The *After* column is what it shows once you have broken the upstream input and updated every step.

|---|---|---|

### T-C10 Actuated linkage (chain 5)

**Chain.** Ejector slider-crank (`mech.mechanism`) → Replacement cylinder; Replacement cylinder (`fp.cylinder`) → Emergency stroke accumulator. Pack template **Actuated linkage** (`pack.actuated-linkage`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\actuated-linkage\README.md`.

**The link into the cylinder is a kinematic proxy** (DB-04). A cylinder-actuated linkage runs at about constant cylinder speed, not at constant crank speed, so the crank's peak slide speed and peak force are what the cylinder must reach and carry, not what it would reproduce through the stroke. The cylinder's flow field is blank: its required extend speed `v_req` (linked) gives the flow. The stroke is typed (200 mm, the slide's stroke).

| Member | Template | Checks when created |
|---|---|---|
| Ejector slider-crank | Mechanism kinematics: four-bar, slider-crank, cam | 3 PASS |
| Replacement cylinder | Cylinder and actuator circuit | 6 PASS |
| Emergency stroke accumulator | Accumulator sizing (gas law) | 2 PASS |

**Values that flow down each link.** The *Before* column is what the pack shows when created. The *After* column is what it shows once you have broken the upstream input and updated every step.

| Link | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Ejector slider-crank `output_motion` → Replacement cylinder `v_req` | 331.325 mm/s | 496.987 mm/s |
| Ejector slider-crank `follower_force` → Replacement cylinder `F_push` | 3.05264 kN | 3.11844 kN |
| Ejector slider-crank `follower_force` → Replacement cylinder `F_pull` | 3.05264 kN | 3.11844 kN |
| Replacement cylinder `dV` → Emergency stroke accumulator `dV` | 0.251327 L | 0.251327 L |
| Replacement cylinder `p` → Emergency stroke accumulator `p_2` | 100 bar | 100 bar |

### T-C12 Envelope moisture (chain 7)

**Chain.** Room heat loss (`bs.room-heat-loss`) → Wall condensation; Room air state (`bs.psychrometrics`) → Wall condensation. Pack template **Envelope moisture** (`pack.envelope-moisture`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\envelope-moisture\README.md`.

**What is linked.** B4's U-value of the first element (`U_1`) is the Glaser step's `U_ref`. B2's dry-bulb (`t_1_out`) and relative humidity (`phi_1`) are the Glaser step's internal temperature and humidity. The wall's layers are **typed in both** the heat-loss and the Glaser steps; no per-layer link exists. The Glaser step warns (`u-ref-mismatch`) when its own U-value departs from B4's by more than 2 %. That guard compares the thermal resistance only: it cannot see a swapped layer order or a wrong vapour resistance factor. The monthly climate, the surface resistances, the critical humidity and the condensate limit are your records. **The example's climate is invented.** B4's layer table has no row editor in the form (D-37), so this script breaks the chain at B2; the automated test revises a B4 layer instead (`EnvelopeMoistureChainTests`).

|---|---|---|
| Room heat loss | U-values and room heat loss | 1 PASS |
| Room air state | Psychrometrics | no check |
| Wall condensation | Glaser condensation (interstitial and surface) | 3 PASS |

| Link | Before | After |
|---|---|---|

| Room air state `t_1_out` → Wall condensation `theta_int` | 21 degC | 21 degC |
| Room air state `phi_1` → Wall condensation `RH_int` | 50 % | 60 % |

### T-C13 Cold pipe (chain 8)

**Chain.** Chilled water pipe (`bs.pipe-pump`) → Pipe insulation; Plant room air (`bs.psychrometrics`) → Pipe insulation. Pack template **Cold pipe** (`pack.cold-pipe`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\cold-pipe\README.md`.

**What is linked.** B1's design temperature of the line (`T_line`, the coldest fluid temperature, not the mean `T` the pipe step takes its properties at) is B9's process temperature; B1's first-segment outside diameter (`OD_1`, from the optional `OD` column of its segments table) is B9's inside diameter; B2's dry-bulb, dew point and relative humidity are B9's ambient temperature, dew point and `RH_a`. The heat-gain limit `q_max_m` is omitted from the example so the dew-point margin is the one criterion. The pipe step's own pump and pressure checks are unaffected by the insulation: nothing is fed back.

### T-C14 Pipework supports (chain 9)

**Chain.** Pipe expansion and anchor force (`bs.pipe-expansion`) → Anchor post frame; Anchor post frame (`struct.frame-2d`) → Anchor post base plate. Pack template **Pipework supports** (`pack.pipework-supports`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\pipework-supports\README.md`.

**What is linked.** B8's `anchor_force` is the first load row (a table cell) of the S2 post frame; S2's base reactions are S6's actions, as in the steel-support pack. B8's moment is **not** carried to the post (it is the guided-cantilever root moment, not an applied one). The links depend on S2's numbering: node 1 the column base (fixed), node 2 the anchor, member 1 the post, the first load row the thermal force. The holding-down bolt data are typed (no bolt-check step). B8's allowable range and screening limit are your records; the example's are invented. **The anchorage (concrete and anchor design) is done in Hilti PROFIS: the file attached to the base-plate step in the shipped example is a placeholder.** S6 demands an independent check (DA-12).

### T-C15 Electronics enclosure (chain 10)

**Chain.** Power trace (`elec.pcb-trace`) → Board load budget; Board load budget (`elec.load-budget`) → Enclosure temperature; Enclosure temperature (`elec.enclosure-heat`) → Cover latches, Cover plate. Pack template **Electronics enclosure** (`pack.electronics-enclosure`). README: `docs\worked-examples\packs\electronics-enclosure\README.md`.

**What is linked.** E7's trace loss (`trace_Ploss`) is a cell of E6's load table (a table-cell link); E6's heat (`Phi`) is E2's additional load; E2's internal temperature is M8's temperature of use; E2's breather-limited pressure is S9's cover pressure `q`, and, times the cover area, M8's required latch force `F_req`. The enclosure is sealed with a pressure-compensation breather, so the pressure difference is the smaller of the gas-law value and the breather's cracking pressure; with the breather governing, a revised trace does not move the pressure or the force. The latches' and cover's material values are **typed for 60 °C**, the enclosure's limit; they do not follow the links. The trace's board ambient (60 °C), the supply's loss row and the latch count (six) are typed.

| Member | Template | Checks when created |
|---|---|---|
| Power trace | PCB trace width (IPC-2221 legacy) | 1 PASS |
| Board load budget | Load budget, PSU, transformer and battery | no check |
| Enclosure temperature | Enclosure heat dissipation and fan sizing | 1 PASS |
| Cover latches | Snap-fits: cantilever, annular, torsion | 3 PASS |
| Cover plate | Plate bending | 2 PASS |

**Values that flow down each link.** The *Before* column is what the pack shows when created. The *After* column is what it shows once you have broken the upstream input and updated every step.

| Link | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Power trace `trace_Ploss` → Board load budget `loads` row 1 (`P`), a table-cell link | 0.00150734 kW | 0.00217056 kW |
| Board load budget `Phi` → Enclosure temperature `P_add` | 73.0073 W | 73.6706 W |
| Enclosure temperature `T_int` → Cover latches `T_use` | 56.8771 degC | 57.0655 degC |
| Enclosure temperature `F_seal` → Cover latches `F_req` | 300 N | 300 N |
| Enclosure temperature `dp_seal` → Cover plate `q` | 1 kPa | 1 kPa |

| # | Action | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|

## 4. Unconfirmed values list

A KAT is **Unconfirmed** when its expected values could not be verified against the primary source. Each is still run by the test suite against the repository's own computation. This list is your verification checklist. There are **13** Unconfirmed KAT cases over the 28 templates of Addendum A and the base set: 10 in Addendum A and 3 in the base set (U-01 to U-13). Addendum B adds 16 more, six of them also Secondary (section 4.5, U-14 to U-29). That makes **29** in all. I counted them from the `CalcReferenceStatus.Unconfirmed` tags in the code. The "location text" is quoted from each case's `CalcReferenceSource`.

### 4.1 Unconfirmed test cases (grep `Unconfirmed`)

**U-01 M4 `mech.shaft` — case `ka-shigley-7-1`** (Shoulder fatigue: 1260 lbf·in reversed bending, 1100 lbf·in steady torque, d = 1.100 in (Shigley Ch. 7 example))

- Source to check: Budynas & Nisbett, Shigley's Mechanical Engineering Design.
- Edition: 10th ed., Ch. 7, Example 7-1.
- Location text in the repository: Data of the example converted to SI exactly: M 1260 lbf·in = 142.361 N·m, T 1100 lbf·in = 124.283 N·m, d 1.100 in = 27.94 mm, S_ut 105 kpsi = 723.949 MPa, S_y 82 kpsi = 565.370 MPa; machined, R 0.99, K_t 1.68, K_ts 1.42, q 0.85, q_s 0.92; computed here (python3, independent): k_a 0.78769, k_b 0.86831, k_e 0.81389, S_e 201.50 MPa (29.23 kpsi), σ_a 104.91 MPa, τ_m 40.23 MPa, n_f (DE-Goodman) 1.621, n_y 4.489. The book's printed answers are to be compared by the owner against their copy; K_t, K_ts, q and q_s are those of the earlier version of this case and are equally to be checked
- Expected values: `S_e` = 201.4997 MPa (relative tolerance 1e-5); `n_f_min` = 1.620988 (relative tolerance 1e-5); `n_y_min` = 4.488973 (relative tolerance 1e-5).
- What to do: find the example in your copy. Compare its inputs and printed answers with the values above. Note any difference. Result: (blank)

### 4.5 Addendum B: Unconfirmed and Secondary test cases (grep `Unconfirmed`, `Secondary`)

Addendum B adds **16** Unconfirmed KAT cases, numbered U-14 to U-29: S7 four (U-14 to U-17), M7 three (U-18 to U-20), E7 two (U-21, U-22), F2 one (U-23), F3 two (U-24, U-25) and S9 four (U-26 to U-29). **Six are also Secondary** (U-20 to U-25). A Secondary case takes its value from a secondary source: a solutions manual, a website, course notes, a vendor example or a number that Addendum B itself quotes. It does not take it from the primary publication (§B4). Each case is still run against the repository's own computation, as in section 4.1. I counted both tags from the code (`CalcReferenceStatus.Unconfirmed` and `Secondary: true`).

M8, M9, E8, E9, B7, B8, B9 and F1 carry **no** Unconfirmed case: their cases are analytic and Confirmed. Section 4.8 still lists the sources that Addendum B names for them and that nobody pinned. The S8 case (PDHonline S106, Secondary) is not in this tree; it joins this list when B-5 merges (T-S8).

Where the repository records no edition for a case, the entry says so. Write down the edition you hold in the **Result** line.

**U-14 S7 `struct.ltb-interaction` — case `ka-annex-b-class2`** (Annex B, Table B.2, class 2: N = 1200 kN with M_y = 200 kN·m (ψ = 0.5) and M_z = 15 kN·m)

- Source to check: Eurocode 3: Design of steel structures — Part 1-1: General rules and rules for buildings (EN 1993-1-1), Annex B, Table B.2 (interaction factors) with the equivalent uniform moment factors table of Annex B.
- Edition: EN 1993-1-1:2005+A1:2014 (UK: BS EN 1993-1-1 with its National Annex).
- Location text in the repository: Analytic from the Annex B expressions as implemented, recomputed in python; the coefficients of Tables B.1 and B.2 are typed from the published expressions and are to be checked against the owner's copy of the standard. Same section, 6.3.2.3 (curve d), C₁ = 1.31, M_cr = 521.515 kN·m, λ̄_LT = 1.194575, χ_LT = 0.457851; C_my = C_mLT = 0.8, C_mz = 1.0; n_y = 0.1, n_z = 0.50548: k_yy = 0.848513, k_yz = 0.6·k_zz = 0.960284, k_zz = 1.600473, k_zy = 0.917840; equations 6.61 = 0.94775, 6.62 = 1.24809
- Expected values: `C_1` = 1.31 (relative tolerance 1e-12); `Mcr` = 521.5153 kN*m (relative tolerance 1e-6); `chi_LT` = 0.457851 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `k_yy` = 0.848513 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `k_yz` = 0.9602837 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `k_zy` = 0.91784 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `k_zz` = 1.600473 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `u_61` = 0.9477492 (relative tolerance 1e-6); `u_62` = 1.248094 (relative tolerance 1e-6).
- What to do: Check the pairing of table and case first. The repository names Table B.2 for class 2 and Table B.1 for class 3. As I recall their titles, one table covers members susceptible to torsional deformations and the other members not susceptible; confirm that in your copy. A member that can buckle laterally is a susceptible one. Say which table your copy applies, then compare the k_yy, k_yz, k_zy and k_zz expressions and the C_my, C_mz and C_mLT values for ψ = 0.5 term by term with the typed ones. Result: (blank)

The working points, E, ρ and S_e are not part of the problem. To be checked against the owner's copy of the book and its solutions manual.
- Expected values: `C` = 11.4 (relative tolerance 1e-9); `k` = 1.3142 N/mm (relative tolerance 1e-4); `k_c` = 1.1174 (relative tolerance 1e-4); `L_s` = 35 mm (relative tolerance 1e-9); `F_c` = 167.86 N (relative tolerance 2e-4); `u_c` = 1 (relative tolerance 1e-4).

### 4.6 Secondary-source cases at a glance

| Case | Secondary source the value came from | Primary source to ask for |
|---|---|---|
| U-20 M7 `ka-shigley-10-3` | The solutions manual for problem 10-3 | The problem statement in the book itself, 10th ed. |
| U-21 E7 `ka-published-3a-10k-1oz` | Trace-width calculators and PCB guides, as Addendum B quotes them | IPC-2221, your revision |
| U-22 E7 `ka-published-3a-10k-1oz-internal` | The same | The same |
| U-23 F2 `ka-parker-hy10-4004` | The HY10-4004/UK example, as Addendum B quotes it | The Parker catalogue itself |
| U-24 F3 `ka-parker-quadratic` | The section 3.5 relation, as Addendum B quotes it | The Parker O-Ring Handbook ORD 5700, your edition |
| U-25 F3 `ka-stretch-rule` | The PTD 5705 stretch rule, as Addendum B quotes it | Parker PTD 5705, your edition |

### 4.7 Worked examples tagged Unconfirmed (Addendum B)

| Calculator | Worked example | What is unconfirmed |
|---|---|---|
| S7 | `struct.ltb-interaction/ub-457x191x98-beam-column` (400 kN with end moments 340 and 170 kN·m, ψ = 0.5; M_b,Rd 539.3 kN·m, 6.62 at 0.931) | The Annex B coefficients (Table B.2 plastic column, C_my = C_mLT = 0.8, C_mz = 1.0) and the section properties taken from the S1 example (UB 457×191×98: check them against the Blue Book). `STATUS.md` words the first as "from memory". |

The other Addendum B worked examples are analytic and carry no Unconfirmed tag. The pack worked examples (section 3.C) use invented data. The cable tables inside the hydraulic power unit example are synthetic test values, labelled as such on the sheet.

## 5. Licensed-record setup

**Rule (§A5).** No BS 7671 or IET table, BS EN 60204-1 table, IEC 60364 table, CIBSE or MCS design-data table, Eurocode National Annex table beyond small factor sets, SKF or Schaeffler aISO diagram, Peterson chart or Roark table ships in source, seeds, fixtures or KATs. **No standards-derived reference record ships with the product at all.** Addendum B adds four generic, indicative seed materials (W3) and nothing else. You enter the values from your own licensed copy or from manufacturer datasheets. This pack lists no table values.

**What a record holds.** Kind, title, revision, source title, edition **and amendment** (record keys must include the amendment, because sources disagree on what an amendment changed), table or clause, licence holder, who entered it (the signed-in person), a **licensed source** flag, and the values. A record is never edited: a change saves the next revision. A calculation pins the revision it used. A record revised after use makes the calculation stale ("Apply the record again"), at every depth of a chain, and a pin to a revision that does not exist counts as stale.

**When to tick *Licensed source*.** Tick it for values copied from a standard or guide you hold under licence (BS 7671, BS EN 60204-1, CIBSE, a National Annex table). Whether to tick it for manufacturer datasheet values depends on the manufacturer's terms. That is your call (section 7).

### 5.1 Records to populate before testing against UK practice

A new sheet opens with every record-kind table empty and some record-kind values at the template default. The sheet warns `<symbol>: a tabulated value not taken from a reference record` until you apply a record.

| Calculator | Record kind | Fills | Enter from |
|---|---|---|---|
| **E4** | `cable.ampacity` | `ampacity` table (cross-section against I_t) | Your licensed table for the installation method and insulation. Machine wiring (default context) uses your BS EN 60204-1 copy; the installation context uses your BS 7671 copy. **Required**: E4 is blocked without it, and so is the motor-circuit pack. |
| **E4** | `cable.correction-factors` | `C_a`, `C_g`, `C_i` | Your licensed ambient, grouping and thermal-insulation factors for the same method. |
| **E4** | `device.ratings` | `ratings` table | The preferred ratings of the device range: manufacturer datasheet or product standard. **Required** as above. |
| **E5** | `conductor.material` | `rho_20`, `alpha`, `Q_c`, `beta`, `theta_i`, `theta_f` | Material constants (IEC 60228 / IEC 60949 values) and the insulation's initial and final temperatures. Form defaults give the copper KAT; check them against your source. |
| **E5** | `device.characteristic` | `curve`, `I2t_let`, `I_cn` | Manufacturer curve and datasheet for the exact device (fuse or MCCB). MCBs B, C and D need no curve. |
| **B4** | `climate.design-temperature` | `theta_e` | The location's external design temperature from your own licensed or published source, with its edition. |
| **B4** | `material.conductivity` | the whole `layers` table (λ column) | Manufacturer's declared value or your licensed reference. |
| **B4** | `thermal.bridging` | `y_tb` | Your own source or a bridging calculation. |
| E6 | `psu.derating` | `f_derate` | PSU manufacturer's derating curve at the ambient and altitude. |
| E6 | `battery.factors` | `DoD`, `eta_b`, `k_temp`, `k_age`, `k_p`, `H_rate` | Battery manufacturer's data. |
| M5 | `bearing.ratings`, `bearing.factors`, `bearing.aiso` | `C`, `C_0`; `e_lim`, `X_1`, `Y_1`, `X`, `Y`, `X_0`, `Y_0`; `a_iso`, `kappa` | Manufacturer catalogue and life tool for the exact designation. |
| M4 | `shaft.material`, `shaft.marin`, `shaft.limits` | `S_ut`, `S_y`, `E`; `k_a`; `theta_max`, `y_max` | Material certificate; your edition of the fatigue reference; bearing and gear data. |
| S4 | `ec3.national-annex`, `steel.strengths` | `gamma_M2`, `gamma_M0`, `k_e1`, `k_e2`, `k_p1`, `k_p2`; `f_y`, `f_u` | National Annex in use; product standard for the grade. |
| S5 | `en1990.factors` | `gamma_G_sup`, `gamma_G_inf`, `gamma_Q`, `xi`, `psi` | UK National Annex (NA.A1.2(A)–(C)), or the EN 1990 Annex A1 recommended set as a separate, labelled record. |
| S6 | `ec2.national-annex`, `ec3.national-annex`, `ec3-1-1.national-annex`, `joint.friction`, `steel.strengths` | `alpha_cc`, `gamma_c`; `gamma_M2`; `gamma_M0`; `C_fd`; `f_y` | National Annexes in use; grout data. |
| B5 | `fluid.properties` | `cp_h`, `cp_c` | A property table or B2 at each stream's mean temperature. |
| B6 | `emitter.rating`, `fluid.expansion` | `n`; `e_fluid` | Emitter manufacturer's catalogue (BS EN 442 rating); glycol supplier's data. |

Kinds are taken from the templates' `RecordKinds` in the code. B4's `surface.resistances` kind of the first draft no longer exists: the surface resistances are now conventional values chosen by the new `flow` column. M6 and the base calculators draw on no record kind.

### 5.2 How to enter a record

1. Open the worksheet, import any table inputs, type the single values, and **Save as calculation**. The records panel shows only for a saved draft whose template declares record kinds.
2. In the panel pick the record kind. Fill Title, Source title, Edition and amendment, Table or clause and Licence holder. All five are required. Tick **Licensed source (never exported)** where it applies.
3. Press **Save inputs as record**. The record takes the form's current values for that kind's symbols, and the inputs now cite it.
4. On another draft of a template that uses the same kind, pick the record under **Take values from** and press **Apply record**. The inputs take the values and cite the record as their source, with the edition as the revision.

The record form always saves a new record at revision 1. Later revisions exist only in the Library service and its tests.

### 5.3 How the licence lock behaves

Grounded in `CalcInstanceService.Records.cs`, `CalcCsv.cs`, `CalcInputJson.cs`, `CalcSheetBuilder.cs` and their tests.

| Path | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| **Export inputs (CSV)** (worksheet) | Passes through `WithholdLicensedAsync`. An input whose source is a licensed record keeps its symbol and its record pin, loses its value, and reads `record:<id>@<rev> · licensed value withheld`. Unlicensed values export. A table is exported cell by cell with each cell's unit and source (so table-cell links round-trip); a table pinned to a licensed record is written as its own row only, with no rows. |
| **CSV or JSON re-import of a withheld value** | A withheld scalar is **blocked**: `<symbol>: this value was withheld on export (licensed record) and is not in the file; apply the record again, or enter the value from your own copy.` (CSV adds `Row <n>: ` in front.) Nothing is imported until you apply the record again or enter the value. A withheld table comes back empty with a warning, and the validator blocks the run. It never comes back as the template default. |
| `ExportRecordAsync` (the record itself) | Refuses a licensed record with: `Record '<title>' holds values from a licensed source (<source>, <edition>); its values are never exported.` Not reachable from the Desktop; tests only. An unlicensed record exports as CSV with its citation in one quoted cell. |
| Issued sheet, pack sheet, preview and PDF | Rows of a table pinned to a licensed record are withheld: the sheet prints the pin, the record's citation and `rows withheld (licensed record); the values used appear in the working`. A scalar from a licensed record is the value used and stays. That is the user's own calculation, so issuing is allowed. A pin to a revision that does not exist is judged by the record's latest revision. |
| Revision of a licensed record | Stays licensed. A revision cannot clear the flag. |
| A record saved from inputs that cite a licensed record | Becomes licensed, so licensed values cannot be laundered into an exportable record. |
| Calc-pack export, record attachments | No such export path exists today. Any new one must use the same guard (DA-06). |
| Known limit (DA-18) | The lock follows the **provenance**, not the numbers. If you retype a licensed value with another source, it exports. |

Test it with T-REC in section 3.

### 5.4 Records to populate for Addendum B

**Rule (§B5).** Nothing below ships in source, seeds, fixtures or KATs: EN 10270 R_m(d) tables, EN 13906 permissible-stress diagrams, IPC-2152 charts, IET cable and conduit factor tables, Eurocode National Annex values and C₁/C₂ tables beyond small factor sets, ISO 13788 climate data and annex examples, BS 5422 thickness tables, ASME B31.3 allowable stresses and K1, ASME BTH-1 and EN 13155 text and tables, AS568 and ISO 3601 size and gland tables, Parker extrusion charts, and the ISO/TS 13725 method. Enter each from your own copy as a record, with **Licensed source** ticked where the lock applies (section 5.3). The four generic seed materials of W3 are the only data that ship, and they are flagged *indicative*.

**What the "Sheet without it" column means.** *Blocked*: the input has no default and is required, so the bar reads `Blocked — …` until you enter it. *Default, warns*: the sheet opens on a starting value (a conventional or invented one, not a table value from your standards) and warns `a tabulated value not taken from a reference record` until you apply a record. *Optional*: the input has no default and the check that needs it is not run. A table input arrives by **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)** (D-37). Import first, then **Save inputs as record**. The kinds are read from the templates' `RecordKinds` at `5a8ba277`.

## 6. External oracles

Use these to cross-check results. None is a dependency: nothing in the build calls them. Enter the KAT inputs from section 3, then compare.

| Calculator | Oracle | Compare | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| E5 | Free MCB maximum-Zs tables from manufacturers | `Z_s_max` for MCB B, C and D at In | The calculator uses C_min × U_0 / I_a with C_min 0.95 and I_a = 5, 10 or 20 × In. A manufacturer's table may use other factors. Record the difference and the reason. KAT: 32 A type B, 230 V gives 1.3656 Ω. |
| E5 | IET Wiring Matters (March 2023) article; Schneider guide on protective conductors | `Z_s_max` 1.37 Ω; derived k 115 (PVC) and 143 (XLPE) | These are two Unconfirmed KATs (U-items in section 4). |
| M5 | SKF online bearing tool; NTN Bearing Wizard | `L10`, `L10h`, and the a_ISO you then type in | a_ISO comes from the manufacturer's tool; the calculator does not compute it. Record the edition (ISO 281:2007 or 1990) each tool uses. a_1 differs between them (0.64 and 0.62 at 95 %). |
| B4 | MCS heat load calculator (if you have access) | Room `Φ_HL` and `HLC` for the same room | Not available to the author. |
| B4 | OpenHeatLoss | Room totals only | **Black box only.** OpenHeatLoss is AGPL v3. Do not read, copy or port its code. Run it, read its answer, and write the number down. |
| B4 | The older calculator `calc.plane-wall-heat-transfer` | `U_1` | The Library test `RoomHeatLossAgreementTests` (5 tests) compares the two (script T-B4, last step). |
| S6 | Hilti PROFIS | Anchor design from `F_t_Ed` per bolt and `V_b_Ed` per anchor | The worksheet hands anchorage to PROFIS. File the PROFIS PDF with **Attach external report…**. |
| S4 | SCI P363 Blue Book resistance tables | Bolt shear and tension resistances | §A6 asks for agreement within 1 %. These are KATs `ka-p363-m20-88` (Unconfirmed). Values are not reproduced here. |
| B6 | Vessel manufacturer sizing guide (Flamco, Reflex, Zilmet); radiator manufacturer ΔT correction factors | `V_e`; the exponent n and `f_dT` at ΔT 30 K | §A6 lists these as KAT sources. The vessel excludes the water reserve and the safety-valve margin (DA-24), so add them before you compare. |
| E6 | PSU manufacturer derating curve (for example Phoenix Contact QUINT, Siemens SITOP); battery manufacturer data | `f_derate`; capacity factors | `ka-psu-derating` is Unconfirmed. |
| M7 | FED1+ (spring design software), or the MITcalc spring calculators | For `plunger-return-spring`: rate `k` 6.4535 N/mm, solid length `L_s` 33 mm, stress at point 2 `tau_2` 477.68 MPa, surge frequency `f_n` 207 Hz | The tools may correct the stress with another factor and take another solid-length rule. The sheet uses the EN 13906-1 solid length by default. Record both choices for each tool. Wire strength R_m(d) is your record, so enter the same R_m in both. |
| S7 | The SCI/BCSA M_cr tool, or LTBeam (CTICM) | `Mcr` 808.38 kN·m for `ub-457x191x98-beam-column` (UB 457×191×98, 5 m between restraints, ψ = 0.5, C₁ = 1.31, k = 1) | Compare M_cr only. These tools do not use the Annex A or B interaction factors. Check that each tool takes the same C₁ and the same load position. U-14 to U-17 still need your copy of the standard. |
| S8 | MechaniCalc (lug analysis) | To be confirmed when B-5 merges. Compare the PDHonline S106 lug (T-S8 step 7) | A lug tool may use another code basis from BTH-1. Say which. Treat the tool as a black box: run it, read its answer, write the number down. |
| S9 | None named in Addendum B. Use the classical coefficients of U-26, U-28 and U-29 as the check | `w_max` 0.2577 mm (simply supported square, U-26) and 0.07998 mm (clamped square, U-28) | A finite-element check of the worked-example plate is the independent oracle if you have one. |
| B7 | WUFI (Pro or Plus), or Condensa | For `internal-insulation-no-vcl`: `n_start` 11 (the first month of condensation), `Mc_max` 3022.6 g/m², `fRsi` 0.8773 | WUFI is dynamic and Glaser is steady-state, so expect WUFI to differ. The check is that the Glaser result is on the safe side and shows condensation in the same layer. The worked example's climate is invented, so enter that climate in the tool. |
| B9 | NAIMA 3E Plus | For `chilled-water-pipe-condensation`: dew point 22.012 °C, heat gain 9.593 W/m, outer surface 25.274 °C, thickness 13.162 mm (anti-condensation) and 17.814 mm (heat-gain limit) | Match the ambient, emissivity and wind. 3E Plus follows US practice and units. Say whether its surface coefficient is a fixed value or a correlation. The sheet offers both. |
| F2 | HYDAC or Parker accumulator sizing tools | For `clamp-circuit-bladder-accumulator`: `V0` 31.656 L, pre-charge `p0` 81.645 bar (absolute) and selection 32 L; for U-23 the Parker example (29.7 to 29.8 L, 32 L) | Settle gauge against absolute first. Match the gas exponent n (1 isothermal). Above 200 bar the sheet warns that the ideal-gas value is shown and takes a real-gas factor `f_rg` from you. |
| F3 | Parker inPHorm | For `locking-cylinder-piston-seal`: largest stretch 3.5104 %, largest extrusion gap 0.10606 mm, C_pk of squeeze 1.561 | inPHorm holds Parker's own size and gland tables. The sheet holds none, so enter the same ring and gland by hand. U-24 and U-25 depend on your copy of ORD 5700 and PTD 5705. |
| M9 | Linkage (the planar mechanism simulator) | For `carton-tucker-crank-rocker`: smallest transmission angle 48.441°, rocker swing 54.786°, peak output angular velocity 3.5435 rad/s | Enter the same four link lengths, the same ground, the same input speed. A cam has no oracle here. |
| E7 | Saturn PCB Toolkit | For `motor-driver-supply-rail`: `w_min` 0.9079 mm, trace resistance 10.819 mΩ, power loss 270.47 mW; for U-21 and U-22: 1.37 mm external and 3.56 mm internal | The toolkit offers more than one method. Pick the IPC-2221 curve-fit. The sheet implements the legacy curve-fit only, not the IPC-2152 charts. |

The Addendum B rows (M7 to E7) name the tools Addendum B §B10 lists. **I have not run any of them.** For each tool, run it, read its answer and write the number down. Do not copy its tables or code into the product. Record the edition or version of the tool and the method it used, because two tools can disagree for a reason that is not a defect of ours.

## 7. Known limitations and open decisions

Source: `docs/decisions/v0.26.0-DECISIONS.md` (DA-01 to DA-24), the Limitations of each spec under `docs/calcs/`, and the Addendum A sections of `docs/reviews/blue-team.md` and `purple-team.md`. Sections 7.1 to 7.4 hold the Addendum A items and sections 7.5 to 7.7 the Addendum B items. **Your call?** is *Yes* where a decision is yours, *Confirm* where it is recorded and wants your nod, *No* where it is information.

| DA-02 | Addendum A merges into `release/v0.26.0`; afterwards that branch is merged forward into `release/v0.27.0`. v0.27.0 already holds `mech.bearing-life`, `bs.plane-wall` and `struct.fillet-weld`, ported from the legacy modules. One reconciliation is planned. | Confirm. |

A one-person practice signs in a second person. | **Yes.** Is that workable? |

A gG fuse shows the deemed I₂ condition; a semi-enclosed fuse defaults to I₂ = 2·I_n (regulation numbers flagged to check). B4: an element is external when its adjacent temperature is blank or at or below θ_e; the bridge allowance is y_tb·Σ(A·f) over external elements × Δθ. BS EN 12831-1 clause numbers and the CIBSE DHDG name and edition are unverified. | **Yes.** Verify the citations (section 4.4). |

Clause numbers and the α_bc range flagged to check. | **Yes.** Verify the citations. |

The pinion forces in the drive train are typed, not linked. The wet-heating pipework volume is typed (a link back would be a cycle). (Blocks now say what to enter next.) |

Issued and checked calculations stay readable and can be revised onto the current version. No v0.26.0 tag was ever pushed, so no owner data is expected on older versions. | **Yes.** (1) against your BS EN 12828 copy; (2) accept. |

### 7.2 Limitations by calculator (spec pages)

| Calculator | Limitations |
|---|---|
| M4 shaft | Linear elastic; two simple supports; no torsional vibration; first lateral mode only; K_t from records or fit equations, never a digitised chart. |
| M5 bearing | Constant speed per step; a_ISO is the manufacturer's value, not computed; P > 0.5 C warns. κ < 0.1 is a hard FAIL; κ > 4 is treated as 4. An axial load above e with `Y` = 0 is refused. |
| M6 weld group | Elastic, static, fillet welds of one leg size; rigid parts. |
| E4 conductor | Single circuit, one installation method per run; factors applied as a product and entered by you; derived C_a is a temperature-difference approximation; I²t and voltage drop are E5 and E3; a CSA below 0.5 mm² (AWG 21 and finer) cannot link to E5; no tables embedded. |
| E5 fault | TN systems; single earth fault; no motor contribution, arc fault or IT systems; no BS 7671 table embedded; when linked from E4 the device must match. |
| E6 load budget | No harmonic or unbalance assessment; inrush only for the single largest start. |
| S4 connection | Static; categories A and D only; no slip-resistant joints, no moment end plates, no countersunk or long joints. |
| S5 combinations | One scalar effect by superposition; buildings only; no EQU set B/C split, bridges, fatigue or seismic. |
| S6 base plate | Static; uniaxial moment; unstiffened; simplified T-stub model (the web gets no credit in the moment check); `m` and `l_eff` are yours to enter (one `l_eff` for modes 1 and 2); prying is your switch, not computed; anchor shear assumes standard clearance holes and equal sharing; β_j = 2/3 depends on grout conditions; the CHS compression side is half the annulus (approximate); anchorage, pull-out, breakout, edge distances and tension–shear interaction are designed externally in PROFIS; no shear key, weld design or fatigue. |
| B4 room heat loss | Steady state, no gains, no thermal mass; homogeneous layers only (no bridged-layer method); ground-floor U is entered by you; one thermal-bridge allowance per area facing outside; a target applies to external built-up elements only; a warning if Φ_HL ≤ 0; no intermittency or reheat allowance except the DHDG user allowance. |
| B5 heat exchanger | Single phase; constant properties; no pressure drop. Crossflow is approximate and flagged. One side at constant temperature is the `const_side` choice of the C_r = 0 arrangement. |
| B6 sealed system | Closed LTHW below 100 °C; no pipe-loss or emitter-position corrections; Kell density at atmospheric pressure; the vessel excludes the water reserve and the safety-valve margin. |

### 7.3 Accepted limits and residual risks that need your call

From the Residual risk list of `docs/reviews/blue-team.md` (Addendum A), Purple's verdicts and DA-23 and DA-24. No Critical or High finding is open. The status wording of the blue-team page was written before Blue round 2; where round 2 closed an item I say so.

| # | Risk or accepted limit | State | Your call? |
|---|---|---|---|
| RR-1 | The licence lock follows provenance, not numbers. A user can retype a licensed value with another provenance and it will export (DA-18). The import-side twin (P-A-01, a withheld value coming back as the default) was closed in Blue round 2: the import is blocked. | Accepted (DA-18) | **Yes.** |
| RR-2 | "Evidence" provenance was not verified (P-A-02). Closed in Blue round 2: the Library refuses an evidence source that does not point at a file attached to the calculation's own Evidence. The file in the shipped steel-support pack is still a placeholder, so its "6 of 6 PASS" shows the mechanism, not an anchorage design. | Closed; placeholder remains | **Yes.** Keep the placeholder example? |
| RR-3 | A record revised in the same instant as a pack issue is not caught (DA-23). The window is one transaction. The lineage rule can also refuse a link to a calculation still pinned to a superseded revision, and chain depth is not counted through lineage (DA-18). | Accepted | **Yes.** |
| RR-4 | The record scan reads every revision. The record store is small, so the cost is low (DA-23). | Accepted | No. |
| RR-5 | G-A-06 and B-A-14 are Partly. M4's yield check now takes a `T_pk` input, but the coupling and yield checks still use the load's demand torque, not a motor peak you choose. Some checks still print the demand as the utilisation (the PROFIS condition shows "utilisation 0"). Neither gives a wrong PASS on the shipped examples, but you must read the sheet. | Partly | **Yes.** Read-the-sheet acceptable? |
| RR-6 | G-A-14 and B-A-17 are in no brief. Three published-looking cases remain (SCI P363, SKF 6206, UC 203), and several substitution lines read oddly (only the key's torque unit was changed). These are the Unconfirmed items of section 4. | Open | **Yes.** Verify. |
| RR-7 | G-B-08: the B6 vessel excludes the water reserve and the safety-valve margin and cites CIBSE B1, not BS EN 12828 (DA-24 (1)). You must add both. | Accepted | **Yes.** Against your BS EN 12828 copy. |
| RR-8 | G-A-05, G-A-12, G-B-17 (DA-22 stated limits): the pinion forces are typed beside a linked torque; one ULS envelope in the steel-support chain; the B6 pipe volume is typed in the wet-heating pack. | Accepted | Confirm. |
| RR-9 | The shipped worked examples use invented data and placeholder files. They show the method. They are not designs. | Accepted | Confirm. |
| RR-10 | Drafts on an older template version do not run (DA-24 (2)); no tag was ever pushed, so none is expected. | Accepted | Confirm. |
| RR-11 | Thirteen templates open blocked on a new sheet (eleven that used to open runnable, per Purple P-A-05, plus E4 which is blocked until you supply the cable table; DA-23). Round 2 gave each block a next-step message. Pre-filled defaults show source *manual*. | Accepted | **Yes.** |
| RR-12 | Purple ran filtered suites only. The full gate (A9) has not run yet. | Open | No. |

### 7.4 Outstanding work and gaps found while drafting this pack (Addendum A)

- `STATUS.md` now reads "Addendum A: 13/13" and records the §A9 gates as met (Windows CI green on `510f337c`).
- The Desktop test counts of Addendum A (section 1) were static counts. For Addendum B I ran the `CalcSheets` filter headless on Linux (T-AUTO).
- I could not establish how a second person is added and given a sign-in identity (T-IND, T-C5). The mechanism is **Settings → Switch person…**; the people list comes from the People directory.
- Where the Desktop lists Reports and Evidence for an issued sheet was not established. The status line says "listed under Reports"; a comment in the Engineering area says its Reports list was withdrawn for the RC, and that Calculations → Calculation register holds the register of issued sheets. T-00 step 7 asks you to note where you find the sheet.
- The form cannot create a motor-circuit pack (the tables) and cannot make a pack from calculations you built by hand (DA-14).
- The older bracket pack (M2 + M3 + S1) from v0.26.0 is not an Addendum A chain and has no script here; read `docs\worked-examples\packs\bracket-assembly-m2-m3-s1\pack-issue-sheet.txt`.

### 7.5 Decisions (Addendum B)

Source: `docs/decisions/v0.26.0-DECISIONS.md` (DB-01 to DB-07), ADR-0170 to ADR-0172 and the Addendum B section of `docs/build-log/addendum-B-orientation.md`. **Your call?** is *Yes* where a decision is yours, *Confirm* where it is recorded and wants your nod, *No* where it is information.

| ID | Item | Your call? |
|---|---|---|
| DB-01 | Addendum B was queued until Addendum A reached its gates. Its decisions carry the prefix DB-nn. Its "ADR-0165 (or next free number)" became ADR-0170. | No. |
| ADR-0170 | Fluid Power is the fifth calculation discipline and the sixth engineering domain. Its three kinds of object (Circuit, Actuator, Power unit) hold typed attributes and **no rating or design-code check**. Pneumatics (ISO 6358) is out of scope and has no placeholder. No pressure-vessel code check (PD 5500, EN 13445, ASME VIII) and no hydraulic-component rating check exists: F2 reports the bar·litre value against the PSSR Schedule 1 exception and does nothing more. Two kernels are shared, not copied: the gas law (B6, F2) and the tolerance-stack engine (M1, F3, W2). | Confirm. |
| DB-02, DB-03 | Hydraulic power unit chain (F1 → F2 → B1 → E1 → E4, later E3, E5, E9). F1's pressure feeds F2's highest working pressure. B1 takes F1's flow, density and line bore by link, the bore as a cell link, so a re-bored cylinder stales the pressure line. B1 runs at Reynolds number 2327 on Colebrook with a `transition` warning, and its fittings term is flagged as understated below 4000. Neither drop is fed back to F1. E4 is sized on the **candidate motor's nameplate power** (E1 1.4.0 gives `P_rated` from an optional `n_rated` in the `motor.rating` record kind). There is no running-speed fallback. **The example's 1460 rpm nameplate is invented.** F1 judges rod buckling at the stall thrust `p·A_b` with no efficiency and no back pressure. F2 warns when a linked gauge pressure meets the Absolute basis. | **Yes.** Is sizing the cable on the nameplate power right for your jobs, and is the invented nameplate fine as an example? |
| DB-04 | M9 keeps its four ports and adds six (`F_pin_pk`, `T_rms_in`, `n_in`, `P_mean`, `lift`, `F_spring_max`) so chains 4 and 5 link physically. **M9 → F1 is a kinematic proxy**: a cylinder drives at about constant speed, not at constant crank speed. A sweep that reaches a toggle or a slider limit is clipped and its peaks and ports are withheld, not reported unbounded. E9 models a soft starter in current limit and a drive as current sources, takes E5's `I_k3` with c = 1.1 as its source, and offers `I_rated` for E4. `I_start` is for coordination only. | **Yes.** Is the proxy acceptable? |
| DB-05 | Chains 7 to 10 are packs. Envelope moisture: the wall's layers are **typed twice** (B4 and B7), and B7 warns when its U-value departs from B4's by more than 2 %. Cold pipe: B1's coldest temperature and outside diameter, and B2's ambient, dew point and humidity, feed B9. Pipework supports: B8's anchor force becomes S2's first load row; B8's moment is not carried; anchorage stays Evidence. Electronics enclosure: E7's loss is a cell of E6's load table; E2 gives M8 and S9 the breather-limited pressure and force. | Confirm. |
| DB-06 | Chains 1, 4 and 5 after verification. Chain 1 adds E3, E5 and E9: E5's `c_k3` feeds E9's `c_src`; the pump starts **unloaded** (an invented 20 N·m unloaded torque; a test checks that a third of the direct-on-line peak covers it); E5's external loop Z_e of 0.05 Ω is invented (a close-coupled 400 kVA transformer); the dip is a **lower bound**, because only the transformer terminals are in it. Chain 4: M9 feeds M7's working travel and operating frequency by link; the gear ratio is 9.6; **M4 takes the cam's RMS torque as a stand-in** for a fully alternating torque, guarded by a test (n_f 9.00 against 8.97). Chain 5: M9 → F1 `v_req` → F2. | **Yes.** Are the stand-in and the invented values acceptable? |
| DB-07 | W1 and W2 share one hardened assembly (`Tempest.Calc.Import`, ADR-0171, ADR-0172): reads capped at 5 MB, a declared number format (no locale guessing), path-refusal rules, strict CSV with formula-safe export. A CAD expression is **never evaluated**. A scan table is read through your own named column profile. Statistics are never trimmed silently. A fitted normal is pushed into M1 with CadImport or ScanImport provenance, which the verifiers downgrade to Manual when they cannot verify it. Provenance records are capped at 20 and kept while any input cites them. | Confirm. |
| §B2 drop | The W2 PDF table path is **not built**, under the §B2 drop order. A table must be exported as CSV or `.xlsx`. | **Yes.** Is CSV and `.xlsx` enough? |
| B-5 | S8 is not in this tree. It will sit behind `Calc:Features:Lifting` (off by default) with a competent-person attestation and a second Checked signature at Issue. Both are recorded names, not authenticated. | **Yes.** Read section 10 before the flag is ever enabled. |
| D-37 | (carried from Addendum A) No table row editor and no table-cell link in the *Link* row. This now also limits the Addendum B tables: the B7 layers and climate, the B8 route, the E8 cables and route, the F1 hose list. It is why chain 7 is broken at B2 in T-C12 and why T-C6 makes 19 of the 20 links. | **Yes.** |

### 7.6 Limitations by calculator (Addendum B spec pages)

| Calculator | Limitations |
|---|---|
| M7 spring | Linear characteristic. Cold-coiled round wire up to 20 mm. No conical, variable-pitch or rectangular-wire springs. Index outside 4 to 20, and fewer than 2 active coils, only warn. Extension hook fatigue and hook shape beyond two radii are not assessed. Surge is for both ends fixed only. R_m(d), the permissible stress fractions and the endurance strength are your records. The fatigue check is a Goodman line on your endurance strength. |
| M8 snap-fit | Linear beam theory with one secant modulus at the design strain. Nominal strain, without the root-fillet concentration. No creep, relaxation, moisture or impact-speed effect. Rigid mating part. Rectangular cantilever, a thin closed ring and a solid round torsion bar only. One Coulomb coefficient for both faces. A warning when l/h < 5. |
| M9 mechanism | Rigid, planar, no clearance, no friction, one degree of freedom, constant input speed. A translating cam follower only. A linear spring, whose surge M7 reports. No link inertia or gravity beyond the output element. A sweep that reaches a toggle or a slider limit is clipped and its peaks are withheld. A change-point linkage continues on the same branch. |
| E7 PCB trace | A curve-fit for steady current, drawn for 1 to 3 oz copper, up to 35 A, 10 to 100 K rise and 10 mm width. Conservative for multilayer boards. Not a thermal analysis (no planes, vias, neighbouring heat, convection or altitude). IPC-2152 is not implemented. |
| E8 containment | Circular cables by overall diameter. No packing rules, no cable or conduit factors (you enter the space factor). Straight lengths are horizontal. No inclines, vertical runs, rollers or pulleys. One μ for the route. Bends use the capstan equation only. Load shares equally between the cables on one pulling head. |
| E9 motor start | The source impedance should be the one at the minimum fault level. A passive start is a constant impedance at the locked-rotor instant, which is the worst case. Low-voltage three-phase, balanced. The star-to-delta changeover spike is not modelled. Capacitor switching transients and detuning are not assessed. The dip limit is yours. |
| S7 LTB | Doubly-symmetric uniform members between fork supports. No mono-symmetric or tapered members, cantilevers or intermediate restraint. No class 4 effective properties, no shear check and no 6.2.9 check. One moment diagram about y-y. The edition switch changes the printed label only. |
| S9 plate | Thin plates (span/t ≥ 10), small deflections (w/t ≤ 0.5), isotropic, constant thickness. One support type all round. Centred loads only. No elastic edges, no local effects under concentrated loads, no temperature or in-plane loads. |
| B7 Glaser | Steady state in each month on a monthly mean climate. No air movement, liquid transport, moisture storage, rain or solar gain. λ and μ constant. Condensation only at the interfaces between layers. One internal temperature. Not for timber or breathable build-ups (EN 15026) and not a mould-growth assessment. |
| B8 pipe expansion | Screening only. A guided-cantilever beam model with rigid elbows. **No stress-intensification factor**, so the stress at the elbow root can be higher than the beam stress. No pressure or ovalisation effects, no out-of-plane legs, no branches, no support friction, weight, hangers or cold spring. A Z-bend lies in one plane. `Sa` and `K1` are your records. |
| B9 insulation | Steady state, one layer of constant λ. No supports, joints, bridges, air gaps or jacket resistance. Condensation is judged on the outer surface only (B7 handles a wall). The correlation is indicative. The ISO 12241 surface coefficients are not implemented. |
| F1 cylinder | Steady motion only: no acceleration, cushioning, compressibility or line losses (B1 gives the lines). Efficiencies are yours. A fixed-displacement pump. The rod is a solid round column. **ISO/TS 13725 is not implemented.** The orifice is the sharp-edged turbulent relation with your C_d. |
| F2 accumulator | Ideal gas unless you give `f_rg`. One gas pre-charge. No gas-side heat transfer, friction, oil compressibility or leakage. The shock duty is a screening of gas volume. The size list and the pre-charge rules are yours. PSSR applicability is for the competent person. |
| F3 O-ring gland | A plain rectangular groove with a corner radius and a circular ring. Edge breaks, wall angles, surface finish, eccentricity, pressure, friction, compression set and stress relaxation are not modelled. The worst case is a stack of independent ends. The extrusion limit comes from your record. Not a seal maker's design review. |
| W1 CAD import | Reads parameters, not geometry or assemblies. A mapping fills inputs and cells of rows that already exist. Real exports vary by version and locale. One configuration of a design table per import. |
| W2 scan import | Normal fits only. No production order, so the within-σ needs a stated subgroup size. A mapping fills existing stack rows (a measured-dimension row may be added). Deviations need upper ≥ 0 ≥ lower. **No PDF path.** Header spellings are unconfirmed. |
| W3 materials | The four seeds are generic and indicative. Temperature tables are read linearly and never extrapolated. No form panel of its own. |

## 9. Recommended test order

Follow this order. Each step lists the script to run. Stop and record any `F` or `B` before you move on.

| Step | Group | Scripts | Why here |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Build and automated tests | Section 1 and T-AUTO | Confirms the clean clone builds and the suites pass on your machine. |
| 1 | **Common flow and L1 linking** | T-00, then T-L1 parts A to D | Everything else (chains, packs, stale markers) relies on linking, the Saved calculations list and the common flow. |
| 2 | **Quick wins** (A-1) | T-M5, T-S5, T-B5, T-E6, T-B6 | Small, scalar-heavy sheets. Fast to compare. |
| 3 | **Machine** (A-2) | T-M6, T-M4, T-E5 | Table imports and the first deliberate FAIL (M4). |
| 4 | **Connections** (A-3, A-5) | T-S4, T-BC1, T-S6 | Includes the bolt-shear behaviour change and the PROFIS attach step. |
| 5 | **Rooms** | T-B4 including its roof case | The `flow` column and the external-only target. |
| 6 | **Chains** (A7) | T-C1 drive train (with Reopen pack), T-C3 wet heating, T-C4 cooling, T-C5 steel support (needs a second person), T-C2 motor circuit | One new project per chain. Do the drive train first: it covers every pack button. |
| 7 | **Records and independence** (A-4) | Populate records (section 5), then T-REC, T-IND, T-E4 against UK practice | Needs your own licensed copies and a second person. Run T-E4 on the synthetic KAT values first, then on your records. |
| 8 | **Addendum B foundations** | T-REF, T-W3, T-FPD | Shared kernels, the material library and the Fluid Power discipline sit under every Addendum B calculator. |
| 9 | **Addendum B calculators**, discipline by discipline | Mechanical: T-M7, T-M8, T-M9. Electrical: T-E7, T-E8, T-E9. Structural: T-S7, T-S9. Building services: T-B7, T-B8, T-B9. Fluid power: T-F1, T-F2, T-F3 | Each script opens on the new-sheet reading, imports a worked example and compares every output. T-M7, T-S7 and T-B7 cover the most Unconfirmed values. |
| 10 | **Import workflows** | T-W1, then T-W2 | They fill the M1 draft that chains 2 and 3 use. |
| 11 | **Addendum B chains**, one new project each | T-C7 scan-to-seal, T-C8 CAD-driven tolerance, T-C9 mechanism review, T-C10 actuated linkage, T-C12 envelope moisture, T-C13 cold pipe, T-C14 pipework supports (second person for the base plate), T-C15 electronics enclosure, then T-C6 hydraulic power unit (needs your cable tables) | Do T-C9 first: it covers the pack buttons with a blocked step. T-C11 waits for B-5. |
| 12 | **Records for Addendum B** | Populate records (section 5.4), then re-run T-M7, T-S7, T-B7, T-B8, T-B9 and T-F3 on your numbers | Needs your own licensed copies. Run each script on the example values first. |
| 13 | **Your own hydraulic job** | Section 11 | Needs a past job of yours and your E4 tables. |
| 14 | **S8, only after B-5 merges** | T-S8, T-C11, then the section 10 checklist | Leave the flag off until the checklist is done. |
| 15 | **Verification desk work** | Section 4 (29 Unconfirmed KATs in all, 16 of them in Addendum B; unpinned sources; citations) and section 6 (oracles) | Can run in parallel with steps 2 to 13. |
| 16 | **Security spot-checks** | See below | Last, so earlier results are not disturbed. |

| # | Check | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| X1 | Import a CSV whose value cell starts with `=`: copy `symbol,value` and `P,=1+1` to the clipboard, open E4, press **Import inputs (CSV/JSON)**. | `Nothing imported: Row 2: P looks like a spreadsheet formula and was refused.` | |
| X2 | Import a CSV that numbers a table's rows with a gap (for example `loads[1]` and `loads[3]` but no `loads[2]`, on E6). | Nothing imported; a message of the kind `loads: the rows must be numbered 1 to <n> without gaps; row <k> is missing.` | |
| X3 | Import JSON with the same symbol twice. | Nothing imported; the notice names the symbol, says how many times it appears and says to keep one. | |
| X4 | T-REC R5 and R6: licensed values in the CSV export, then re-imported. | Withheld; the re-import is blocked. | |
| X5 | T-E4, T-E5, T-S6 last step and T-IND: creator checks own work. | Refused. | |
| X6 | T-00 steps 6 and 9: edit an issued or superseded sheet. | Not possible: the form is read-only. | |
| X7 | Revise a record-citing calculation after the record changes (service test `Applying_A_Record_Pins_It_And_A_Later_Revision_Makes_The_Calculation_Stale`). | Passes. | |
| X8 | Type a made-up reference with source *evidence* on S6 `profis_ref`. | The anchorage condition does not pass; the Library refuses an evidence source that is not an attached file (test `ExternalEvidenceTests.P_A_02_An_Evidence_Source_Must_Point_At_A_File_Attached_To_The_Calculations_Own_Evidence`). | |
| X9 | T-W1 step 6: a CAD file whose right-hand side is an expression (`[mm]derived=plate_len-spacer_len`). | The preview lists it as read-only text that is **never evaluated**. No input takes its value. | |
| X10 | Make a 6 MB file (`[IO.File]::WriteAllBytes("$w\big.csv", (New-Object byte[] 6291456))`) and pick it in **Choose scan table…** and in **Choose CAD file…**. | Both are refused (the cap is 5 MB). Nothing is applied. Record the message. | |
| X11 | In `groove.csv` (T-W2) change one `Actual` cell to `=1+1` and import it. | The cell is refused as a spreadsheet formula, or the table is. Nothing is applied. Record the text. | |
