| X13 | T-S8 step 4, once B-5 has merged: set the S8 flag from an environment variable. | S8 stays hidden. The flag is read only from `appsettings.json`. | |

## 10. S8 review checklist (before the flag is ever enabled)

**Why this section exists.** S8 designs the lugs and spreader beams that hang a load. A wrong PASS can drop a load on a person. The feature flag `Calc:Features:Lifting` is off by default and stays off until this checklist is signed. The checklist applies to S8 as it merges from B-5. S8 is not in the tree this pack was written against (T-S8), so every row below names what to check, not what the sheet says.

**Who and what.** A competent person (the person LOLER regulation 9 has in mind for a thorough examination, or a chartered engineer with lifting-equipment experience) does the review. The reviewer holds their **own copies** of ASME BTH-1, *Design of Below-the-Hook Lifting Devices* (the 2023 edition or later; the addendum names 2023 or the 2026 edition), and EN 13155, *Cranes: non-fixed load lifting attachments*, with its A1 amendment. The reviewer also holds EN 13001-3-1 (*Cranes — General design — Part 3-1: Limit states and proof of competence of steel structures*), which Addendum B names as the calculation route of EN 13155 mode. The reviewer also holds the Lifting Operations and Lifting Equipment Regulations 1998 (LOLER) and the Provision and Use of Work Equipment Regulations 1998 (PUWER).

**Ground rules.**

- **Cite clause numbers and titles only.** Never copy standards text, tables or figures into this file, into the repository or into a record that is not marked licensed. Write the reviewer's answer as a clause number plus *agrees*, *differs* or *not in my edition*.
- The only BTH-1 clause number Addendum B names is **3-3.3**, for the lug equations. Every other BTH-1 and EN 13155 clause number in the *Clause in your copy* column is left for the reviewer to fill. I did not look any up. Write down the title next to each number you fill.
- Read the spec page of S8 (`docs/calcs/structural/struct.lifting.md`, once B-5 merges) and the sheet's **Show working** for each equation. Hand-calculate each one on the S106 case and on one case of your own.
- A PASS in this checklist means the sheet's equations match your edition. It does not make the program's output a thorough examination. LOLER regulation 9 still needs a competent person's examination of the real equipment.

**A. BTH-1 mode: the equations.** Check each against clause 3-3.3 and its neighbours in your copy.

| # | Check | Expected | Clause in your copy | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | **Design factor N_d.** The sheet takes N_d from a record, by Design Category. Check the category you enter, the stress it divides, and that the sheet refuses to run without a record. | N_d comes only from your record. No default ships. The sheet divides the stress the clause says. | | |
| A2 | **Bearing at the pin hole.** The bearing area, the bearing strength used (yield or ultimate) and the pin-to-hole clearance the check assumes. | The area is the one your clause names. The strength and the factor match your clause. | | |
| A3 | **Tension beyond the hole (net section).** The width used for the net section and the strength it is compared with. | The width and strength match your clause. | | |
| A4 | **Shear-out (tear-out) area.** The planes, the lengths measured from the hole edge and the area that carries the load. | The sheet's area equals the area your clause describes. The sheet does not count the same material in two planes. | | |
| A5 | **Single-plane fracture beyond the hole.** The area, the strength (ultimate) and the factor. | The sheet's expression equals your clause. The S106 value of Addendum B reproduces to its tolerance. | | |
| A6 | **Double-plane shear.** The angle φ and the area it gives, as Addendum B §B6 S8 states them. | The angle and the area equal your clause. | | |
| A7 | **Dishing correction C_r and the ratio D_p/D_h.** The expression, and what happens outside the range of the ratio. | The sheet applies your clause's correction. A ratio outside the valid range gives a hard FAIL, not a warning (§B4: hard FAIL outside the valid range for S8). The ratio limits come from your record. | | |
| A8 | **Effective width limits.** The limit on the effective width `be` and on the edge distance `a`. | The sheet refuses or caps `be` and `a` exactly as your clause does. | | |
| A9 | **Hole and plate geometry.** The minimum thickness, hole position and edge radius `R` rules that your clause sets. | Each rule is checked or warned. None is silently assumed. | | |
| A10 | **Strengths.** F_y and F_u arrive from a W3 material (T-W3) of the grade you use. | The sheet cites the material and its revision. A seed material (indicative) raises its notice. | | |
| A11 | **Units.** The S106 case is in kip and inches. Check the conversion to kN and mm. | 1 kip = 4.4482 kN. 1 in² = 645.16 mm². The converted S106 values reproduce to the case tolerance. | | |

**B. Load direction: sling angle, out-of-plane angle, tilt.**

| # | Check | Expected | Clause in your copy | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | **Sling force from the vertical load.** The sheet resolves the vertical force F_v into the sling force F_s along the sling, for an in-plane sling angle θ and an out-of-plane angle β. | F_s = F_v / (cos θ · cos β). Check the **definition of each angle** on the sheet (from the vertical, or from the horizontal). If the sheet measures from the horizontal, the cosines swap for sines. | | |
| B2 | **Which force the lug is checked for.** | The lug is checked for F_s, in the direction of the sling, and for its in-plane and out-of-plane components. It is not checked for F_v alone. | | |
| B3 | **Out-of-plane load redistribution.** How the out-of-plane component enters the checks of A2 to A5. | The treatment equals your clause. If your clause has a separate out-of-plane check, the sheet has one. | | |
| B4 | **Load shared between lugs.** Two-lug and four-lug lifts and the unequal-sharing factor. | The sheet states how it shares the load. Any factor is a record or an input, never a default. | | |
| B5 | **Angles at their limits.** Try θ = 0, θ near the largest the sheet accepts, and β > 0. | The sheet blocks outside its range. It never divides by a cosine near zero. | | |

**C. EN 13155 mode.**

| # | Check | Expected | Clause in your copy | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **Load cases and factors.** The two load cases (an elastic case and a no-release case up to a stated cycle count) with the factors Addendum B §B6 S8 states. | The factors and the cycle limit equal your edition (A1 amendment included). The cycle limit blocks or warns when exceeded. | | |
| C2 | **Calculation route.** Addendum B names the route of EN 13001-3-1. | The route the sheet cites is the one your EN 13155 points to. | | |
| C3 | **Tilt load case for horizontal beams.** The automatic tilt case, its angle and which loads it moves. | The sheet applies the tilt case on its own for a horizontal beam, with the angle your clause gives. The tilt can be seen in **Show working**. | | |
| C4 | **Working load limit (WLL).** The definition of WLL on the sheet and the marking. | The sheet's WLL is the lifted mass the attachment is rated for, as in your edition. | | |
| C5 | **Proof-load value and marking checklist.** The sheet prints a proof-load value and a marking and certification checklist. | Each marking item exists in your edition. The proof load equals the test load your edition sets. The checklist is text only and claims no certification. | | |
| C6 | **Both modes at once.** | The governing mode is the lower capacity. The sheet names it and shows both utilisations. | | |

**D. Records: nothing ships.**

| # | Check | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Search the source, the seeds, the worked example and the test fixtures of S8 for any BTH-1 or EN 13155 table value or sentence. | None. N_d by Design Category, the ratio limits, the service-class factors and every coefficient table are records you enter. | |
| D2 | Open the spec page and the sheet text. | Each cites clause numbers and titles only. No sentence is lifted from a standard. | |
| D3 | The worked example. | It says it is invented and not a design. It uses made-up record values. | |

**E. Governance at Issue.**

| # | Check | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | The attestation (name, qualification, statement). | Required and not empty. It cannot come from the creator. | |
| E2 | The second Checked signature. | Two different checkers, neither the creator nor anyone who edited an input. | |
| E3 | **What "signature" means here.** The attestation and the second check are **recorded names only**. Nothing authenticates them. The program asks for no password and no certificate, and anyone who can type can type any name (T-S8 step 13). | Decide, in writing, whether your procedure accepts that. If it does not, require a signed record outside the program and file it with the issue. | |
| E4 | The scope wording on the sheet. | It reads "design calculation, not a thorough examination" and carries the LOLER and PUWER scope notes. Check the notes against the regulations: regulation 4 (strength and stability), regulation 7 (marking of lifting equipment) and regulation 9 (thorough examination and inspection). The regulation numbers are mine from memory. Check them. | |
| E5 | The issued sheet and its Evidence. | The PDF names the template version and hash, the mode, every record pin, the attestation and both checkers. | |

**F. The flag.**

| # | Check | Expected | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Where the flag lives. | Only `appsettings.json` next to the executable (T-S8 steps 3 to 5). An environment variable or a command-line switch does not enable it. | |
| F2 | Who may edit that file on each machine. | A named person. A machine that issues real calculations never has the flag on before this checklist is signed. | |
| F3 | With the flag off, what happens to an issued S8 sheet. | You know whether it stays readable. Nothing else can create, import or issue S8. | |

**G. Sign-off.**

| Item | Entry |
|---|---|
| Reviewer, qualification, date | |
| BTH-1 edition and EN 13155 edition (with A1) used | |
| Rows that differ, with the clause number of each | |
| Decision | Enable the flag on: (machines) / Do not enable |

## 11. A real hydraulic test case: one of your own jobs through chain 1

**Why.** Every Addendum B number so far comes from an invented example or from a hand check. Chain 1 (`pack.hydraulic-power-unit`) is the one chain that runs from a hydraulic circuit all the way to the electrical supply. A job you built and commissioned is the first test where a real unit can tell you the program is wrong. This section asks you to run one such job through the chain and to record what differs.

**Which job.** Steven, pick a past power-unit or cylinder job of your own. The best one has:

- a hydraulic circuit diagram and the cylinder, pump and motor datasheets;
- the cable run, protective device and supply details as built;
- a commissioning record with measured values: working pressure, cycle time, motor current, oil temperature, and any voltage dip, nuisance trip or noisy start;
- an accumulator on the emergency stroke, so that F2 has something to size. With a cylinder-only job, run F1 and B1 and leave F2 out.

If you have two jobs, use one that worked and one that gave trouble. The trouble job is the better test of the checks.

**How.** The form cannot create this pack without your cable tables (T-C6 part A). Build the eight calculations by hand, upstream first, as T-C6 part C does, **but import nothing from the invented example**. Type your own job data. Give every input a source other than *manual* (drawing, spec, evidence or your measurement) so that a single calculation can be checked. Enter your own `ampacity` and `ratings` tables for E4 as licensed records (T-REC part 2). Do not use the repository's synthetic tables for a real cable.

**Before you run it.** Write your expectation of each key output in the table below, from the as-built job. Do this first. It stops you tuning the inputs to match.

**Where each input comes from.**

| Step | Inputs from your job |
|---|---|
| F1 Cylinder and pump circuit | Bore, rod, stroke, supply pressure, push and pull loads, required speed or cycle time, pump displacement and speed, efficiencies, hose and pipe sizes and your velocity limits |
| F2 Accumulator | Pre-charge, lowest and highest working pressure, temperatures, the size list of the maker, gauge or absolute basis, the swept volume (it arrives by link from F1) |
| B1 Pressure line | Line length, bore, fittings, oil density and viscosity at the working temperature (the flow, the density and the bore arrive by link, the bore as a typed value in the form) |
| E1 Pump drive motor | Nameplate rated speed (`n_rated`) and torque, rotor inertia, duty. The pump torque and speed arrive by link |
| E4 Motor supply cable | Your cable and device tables, installation method, length, grouping and ambient. The motor's rated power arrives by link |
| E3 Voltage drop | Supply voltage and your drop limit. The current, length and cross-section arrive by link |
| E5 Fault protection | Transformer rating and impedance, measured external loop impedance Z_e, the device curve, the required disconnection time. The cable data arrive by link |
| E9 Motor start | Locked-rotor ratio and starting power factor from the motor datasheet, the starter type (direct, star-delta, soft starter or drive), the dip limit. The fault current, cable and rated power arrive by link |

**Record sheet.** Fill the first columns before the run and the others after. Class each difference as: *program wrong*, *my data wrong*, *model limit* (section 7.6) or *expected*.

| Step | Output | My expectation | Program | As built or measured | Source of that figure | Difference | Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Pump delivery against circuit flow (`u_pump`) | | | | | | |
| F1 | Extend and retract force against the loads | | | | | | |
| F1 | Cycle time `t_cycle` | | | | | | |
| F1 | Line velocities (suction, return, pressure) | | | | | | |
| F1 | Rod buckling utilisation | | | | | | |
| F1 | Shaft torque `T_shaft` and power `P_pump` | | | | | | |
| F2 | Gas volume `V0`, selected size, pre-charge `p0` | | | | | | |
| B1 | Pressure drop in the line and its velocity (note the Reynolds number and any warning) | | | | | | |
| E1 | RMS torque utilisation and `P_rated` | | | | | | |
| E4 | Cross-section, device rating, conductor capacity `I_z` | | | | | | |
| E3 | Voltage drop (volts and per cent) | | | | | | |
| E5 | Loop impedance against its maximum; `I_k3`; disconnection time | | | | | | |
| E9 | Dip at the motor terminals and at the point of common coupling; kvar to correct | | | | | | |

**Run the three what-ifs of T-C6 on your job**, with your own numbers: raise the pressure by 10 %, lengthen the stroke by 20 %, and halve the transformer. Write down which steps go stale and which clear by themselves (the first break stales every step except the pressure line; the second only the accumulator; the third only the motor start). The point is that you can predict the stale list before you look.

**Also record.**

- Which inputs you had to guess or take from a catalogue because no drawing or record held them.
- Every notice, warning and block, with its text. Say which ones told you something you did not know.
- Any check that FAILs where the real unit works, or PASSes where the real unit gave trouble. This is the finding that matters most.
- The links the form made, the links it refused and the stale banners you saw.
- The time the run took, and where it was slow.
- Whether the answer would have changed a decision on the job.

**Keep in mind.** F1 is steady-state: no acceleration, cushioning or compressibility. B1's fittings loss is understated below Reynolds number 4000. E9's dip is a lower bound. F1's rod buckling is a screening (ISO/TS 13725 is not implemented). E4 sizes the cable on the motor's nameplate power, not on the pump's load. The pump is assumed to start unloaded. Use the run to test the program, not as the design basis of the job.
